This study evaluates the conditional value of fusing FY-3E WindRAD data with ERA5 for 12-h short-term typhoon wind vector prediction using matched 2023 samples. We compared ERA5-only, naive, mask-aware, and gated fusion across coverage-sensitivity, band-comparison, and temporal-window experiments. Results show that FY-3E fusion provides conditional numerical benefits rather than consistent improvements. Under the band-specific evaluation, Ku-band gated fusion reduced vector root mean square error (RMSE) from 5.736 to 5.366 m/s and average wind speed error (AWSE) from 3.597 to 3.276 m/s, although these improvements were not statistically significant after multiple-comparison correction. Dual-band gated fusion did not outperform the ERA5-only baseline, and under the common-sample evaluation neither Ku-band nor Dual-band showed a clear advantage. The ±3 h window provided a more favorable balance between sample availability and temporal consistency than the wider ±6 h window. Overall, FY-3E WindRAD data provides complementary information, but its effectiveness depends on coverage, band selection, temporal matching, and fusion strategy.

Abstract (英文): Solar-induced chlorophyll fluorescence (SIF) is a powerful proxy for photosynthesis, yet its quantitative relationship with gross primary productivity (GPP) varies considerably among ecosystems. The underlying mechanisms for this variability, particularly in structurally complex forests, remain poorly understood. Using tower-based SIF and eddy-covariance measurements from two contrasting plantation forests—a drought-tolerant Quercus variabilis and a fast-growing poplar—we investigated how species-specific light-energy allocation strategies influence SIF-GPP relationships and the performance of mechanistic light-reaction models. The key findings are as follows: (1) The photochemical mechanistic model substantially reduced the underestimation of GPP under high-light conditions. A simplified qL-PAR model, derived from leaf-level active fluorescence measurements, produced comparable performance to that of the full photochemical model, i.e., the MLR-NPQ method—a mechanistic light-response model with canopy SIF. (2) The MLR model captured seasonal light-energy partitioning dynamics across two plantations and revealed contrasting light-energy allocation patterns between the two plantation systems. Poplar prioritized photochemical efficiency (ΦP, averaging 0.41) to maximize carbon gain, whereas Q. variabilis invested more energy in NPQ and SIF for a photoprotective “safety-first” strategy. (3) However, MLR models exhibited inconsistent performance. For the poplar plantation, the model achieved higher accuracy (R2 = 0.83 at the daily scale and R2 = 0.65 at the half-hourly scale) than for the Q. variabilis plantation (R2 = 0.65 at the daily scale and R2 = 0.45 at the half-hourly scale). (4) The contrasting ΦP–ΦF relationships provide a physiological explanation for the species-specific performance of the SIF-based GPP models. For poplar, ΦP–ΦF maintained a tight linear relationship across a wide range of moderate-to-high PAR levels. In contrast, for Q. variabilis under high PAR (ΦP = 0.249), NPQ became saturated while SIF continued to rise, leading to a decoupling of the ΦP–ΦF relationship. Our results suggest that the contrasting light-energy allocation patterns observed at the two sites may contribute to the performance discrepancies in canopy-scale SIF-based GPP simulations. Our results collectively validate that a photochemical mechanistic framework advances the process-oriented simulation of photosynthetic energy partitioning in plantation canopies. These findings highlight the potential need to integrate species-specific ΦP–ΦF relationships to better constrain the competitive balance between carbon gain and photoprotective safety—a key uncertainty that must be reconciled for robust GPP retrievals over managed forests but requires further validation. These findings contribute to a mechanistic understanding of SIF–GPP relationships in tall forests and provide a reference for improving vegetation parameterization in regional-scale modeling applications where such ecosystems are represented.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Floods, earthquakes, and landslides may require a multi-satellite synthetic aperture radar (SAR) system to insert new observations after a regular plan has been prepared but before upload. The revised plan must respect visibility, imaging chronology, look-direction switching, orbit resources, strip formation, and plan stability. We propose a multi-relational graph convolutional network with constrained proximal policy optimization (GCN-PPO). Five opportunity relations encode conflict, feasible sequence, cross-satellite competition, demand alternatives, and strip synergy. A three-level policy selects the action category, logical completion unit, and observation opportunity, while feasibility-normalized constraints regulate emergency-service shortfall and plan perturbation. In a 300 s equal-budget experiment, all 750 returned schedules were feasible and free of hard-constraint violations, and 96.4% passed the preregistered timing contract. Under natural termination, GCN-PPO responded in 102.4 s on average, 9.35% of the ALNS time, and met the normalized emergency target in 98.1% of evaluations. Its per-edit perturbation was 31.4% lower than MLP-PPO and 35.9% lower than ALNS. Timely executable schedules support rapid SAR data acquisition for downstream disaster-object detection and emergency monitoring.

Abstract (英文): L-band Vegetation Optical Depth (VOD) retrievals in tropical forest almost always use the τ–ω model, which parameterizes canopy scattering with an effective single-scattering albedo and usually neglects higher-order scattering. This work reports nine months of continuous observations from a four-channel ground-based GNSS-Transmissometry/Reflectometry (GNSS-T/GNSS-R) instrument on the 45 m walk-up tower at the LBA K67 site, Tapajós National Forest, Brazil. Four antennas feed two dual-channel receivers for the direct, reflected (LHCP and RHCP), and canopy-transmitted (RHCP) signals. Three direct observational diagnostics are consistent with a canopy volume-scattering contribution. The transmitted opacity increases with incidence angle but recovers only part of the expected path-length dependence, shows no dependence on the LiDAR-derived canopy height, and the circular polarization ratio departs from the angular decline of a coherent reflection. A joint retrieval recovers the apparent volume-scattering amplitude Gvω0 as a positive residual of the measured return over the modeled coherent two-way ground path. Gvω0 is an effective scattering residual, not the τ–ω single-scattering albedo ω. For GPS L1 it decreases from 0.85[0.59,1.19] to 0.41[0.28,0.57] through the dry-to-wet transition. This seasonal magnitude is an internal-consistency result from the measurement chain, without independent validation. It depends on two conservative choices. A smooth-surface soil model maximizes the subtracted coherent ground return. A two-term decomposition omits the canopy–ground interaction paths.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ground-based Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) vertical displacements provide an independent measure of hydrological loading, but their ability to resolve terrestrial water storage (TWS) variations in arid inland basins remains uncertain. This study compared vertical displacements from 15 GNSS stations within and around the Heihe River Basin with loading displacements derived from satellite-based GRACE and model-based Noah TWS anomalies. After removing non-hydrological signals from GNSS series, correlations among the network-mean vertical displacement for GNSS-GRACE, GNSS-Noah, and GRACE-Noah were 0.48, 0.49, and 0.74, respectively. Mean station-level correlations for the same comparisons were lower (0.38, 0.35, and 0.60, respectively), with weaker agreement within the basin interior. Time-series decomposition showed that GNSS captured inter-annual TWS variability more consistently than the seasonal cycle of TWS. GNSS generally peaks earlier than GRACE in the south but later in the north. Noah interannual variations diverged from both GNSS and GRACE, suggesting limitations in the model’s representation of arid-region hydrological processes. Overall, GNSS provides an independent constraint on long-term TWS variability and a diagnostic of land surface model performance. These findings support multisource monitoring of arid-basin water storage while highlighting the need for improved scale matching and in situ validation.

Abstract (英文): The future trajectory of tropical vegetation greening remains highly controversial, and the dry-season dynamics of tropical rainforests represent a critical knowledge gap. To advance understanding, this study integrates a suite of multisource satellite vegetation indexes (VIs) and process-based ecosystem models, aiming to comprehensively assess long-term trends in both canopy greenness and photosynthetic capacity during the dry season, and to critically evaluate the inter-product discrepancies in trend detection. Furthermore, we applied partial least squares regression (PLS) to isolate the relative contributions of major climatic factors to dry-season vegetation change. Results showed that great discrepancies in trends of vegetation greenness and productivity from different VIs, presenting that, dry-season canopy greenness (leaf area index, normalized difference vegetation index), and vegetation photosynthesis (sun-induced chlorophyll fluorescence), consistently displayed an increasing trend in tropical rainforest areas during the dry season over the past four decades at the average rate of 5.0 × 10−3 m3·m−3·yr−1, 0.3 × 10−3 yr−1, and 0.9 × 10−3 W·m−2·nm−1·sr−1·yr−1, however, vegetation productivity (gross primary productivity, and vegetation optical depth with C, X, and Ku bands) showed large decreasing trends at a rate of −5.0 × 10−3 gC·m−2·yr−1, −0.7, −0.1, and −0.7 × 10−3 yr−1, respectively, and it was occupied with great spatial disparities that the initial increasing GPP in Congo weakened over time, shifting around the early 2000s from a positive trend (1982–2002) to a negative trend thereafter (2002–2018), while this trend was overwhelmingly contrary to GPP in Southeast Asia rainforest. Multisource remote-sensed VIs did not consistently support a significant greening tendency across the tropical rainforests during the dry season. However, process-based models consistently predicted a robust increasing trend of LAI and GPP in the dry-season tropical rainforests throughout the 21st century at the rate of 0.9 × 10−3 m3·m−3·yr−1 and 2.4 × 10−3 gC·m−2·yr−1. CO2, vapor pressure deficit (VPD), temperature, and solar radiation, defined as the heat-related variables, played a largely negative role in contributing to vegetation growth in dry-season tropical rainforests from different VIs, and the water-related factors, including PRE, SM, and ET, positively contributed to vegetation growth. In terms of contributions, rising CO2 concentration, temperature, and VPD were detected as three of the most important drivers of changes in tropical rainforests, with a contribution degree of 45%, 25%, and 20%, respectively. This study emphasizes that a comprehensive evaluation framework for data assimilation between multisource remote-sensed data and process-based model data in the tropics, which helps reduce uncertainties induced by intensifying climate change and the absence of observation stations, needs to be further improved and refined.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A dataset of uncompressed synthetic aperture radar (SAR) raw data has been acquired with Sentinel-1C and Sentinel-1D, providing, for the first time, complex raw data representative of the instrument signal prior to lossy onboard compression. Comprised of 65 Level-0 products and made openly available to the community, it enables a direct assessment of onboard processing algorithms with measurements unaffected by the flexible dynamic block adaptive quantization (FDBAQ) nominally applied by Sentinel-1. A detailed description of the data is presented together with an analysis of the achieved Sentinel-1 FDBAQ compression performance, obtained by emulating the compression/decompression process. The impact of the FDBAQ quantization on SAR radiometry and image quality is analyzed in both the single-look complex (SLC) and raw data domains. The released dataset and derived metrics are intended to establish a quantitative reference for evaluating future SAR compression schemes against the nominal Sentinel-1 FDBAQ implementation, supporting the development of onboard processing and compression-aware machine-learning algorithms.

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) scanning and mobile laser scanning provide detailed three-dimensional observations for forest inventory and structural monitoring for which point-wise separation of ground, understory, woody components, and foliage is fundamental. Modern segmentation pipelines can produce multiple predictions from different feature configurations, training objectives, random seeds, or network architectures. However, equal and fixed-weight fusion implicitly assume stable source quality, although the relative reliability of these predictions can vary across semantic classes and sensing conditions, leading to error propagation and negative transfer. We therefore formulate multi-source prediction integration as a calibration-supported class-wise reliability decision problem and propose the unified class-wise reliability gate (UCRG). The UCRG measures global and class-wise source reliability from labeled calibration data, uses shrinkage to stabilize class-specific estimates when labels are scarce, and conditionally performs soft fusion or hard source selection according to the reliability gap. An optional calibration-guided confusion-pair refiner further targets residual ambiguity between semantically similar classes. All the reliability estimation and parameter selection processes are performed without query labels. Experiments on FOR-instance, EvoMLS, and Lin3D cover UAV and mobile laser scanning together with cross-dataset transfer. Using one shared parameterization across the three primary protocols, the UCRG obtains 70.68% mIoU and records two negative-transfer episodes across the six evaluated episodes compared with six for fixed-weight fusion. When the gate parameters are selected solely from the available calibration support in each deployment, the UCRG reaches 59.20% mean mIoU in the PTv3-augmented Lin3D candidate pool. These results show that reliability-guided decisions can exploit class-dependent complementarity across heterogeneous frozen predictors and sensing conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban trees provide important ecosystem services; however, municipal inventories used to characterize urban-forest composition remain incomplete and inconsistent among cities. We developed a multimodal deep-learning framework that combined municipal inventories with tree-centered National Agriculture Imagery Program aerial imagery, airborne LiDAR canopy height, Sentinel-2 time series and phenology, Google Satellite Embeddings, and daily weather. Beginning with 5.43 million records from 60 U.S. cities, quality-control and data-availability screening retained 1.85 million records representing 579 taxa across 36 cities. Because many species and genera had overlapping remote-sensing signatures, a supervised discrimination model identified 11 intermediate groups that were subsequently collapsed into six remote-sensing-derived taxonomic groups. On an independent test set of 161,508 records, the final pruned ensemble achieved 0.793 accuracy, 0.772 macro-F1, and 0.792 weighted-F1. Group-specific F1 ranged from 0.705 to 0.867 among trees with LiDAR-derived vegetation height ≥ 2 m. Gate weights emphasized high-resolution NAIP-based inputs, whereas ablation and single-modality analyses showed that Sentinel-2 and weather time series supplied important complementary temporal information. Mean accuracy and macro-F1 declined to 0.685 and 0.478 when target cities were withheld but remained substantially higher than for models trained using individual cities. This framework supports scalable classification of individual urban trees into broader remotely distinguishable groups, not species-level identifications or ecological functional types. Sentinel-2 measurements provide contextual information at 10 m resolution rather than individual-crown observations.

Abstract (英文): Protected Areas (PAs) are essential for global biodiversity conservation, yet assessing their management and conservation effectiveness remains a formidable challenge. While remote sensing (RS) enables synoptic, large-scale monitoring, existing reviews are often fragmented—either decoupled from PA governance contexts or confined to isolated threat mappings. To bridge this gap, this review proposes a dual-dimensional analytical framework integrating management effectiveness and conservation effectiveness. Based on the literature retrieved via a systematic Boolean search in the Web of Science, we first establish a multi-source RS technology stack covering space-borne optical, radar, nighttime-light, LiDAR and cloud-computing platforms. We then review RS applications within the two dimensions. For management effectiveness, we trace the progression from single-disturbance tracking to integrated pressure assessments using counterfactual matching designs. For conservation effectiveness, we synthesize advances in biodiversity dynamics, ecosystem pattern integrity, quality evaluation, and ecosystem service quantification. Our review demonstrates that RS has become a major component of large-scale PA evaluation, revealing measurable successes while highlighting the risk of overestimating conservation outcomes when relying solely on greenness-based proxies. Persistent challenges include the lack of standardized, biome-specific assessment frameworks and the disconnect between biophysical monitoring and socio-ecological drivers. Addressing these gaps requires unified evaluation systems aligned with the Kunming-Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework, AI-driven multi-sensor fusion, and the operationalization of RS into routine PA governance to support the “30 × 30” target and ecological product value realization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Transformer-based methods have achieved remarkable performance in hyperspectral image (HSI) classification. However, their ability to learn discriminative multi-scale spatial–spectral features remains limited by the single-granularity token-to-token interactions in conventional self-attention and the imbalanced modeling of spatial and spectral information. To address these limitations, we propose a multi-granularity weight transformer (MGWT) network comprising a spatial–spectral grouped parallel residual tokenizer (S2-GPRT) and a multi-granularity attention (MGA) module. Specifically, the S2-GPRT partitions the spectral bands into groups according to their physical characteristics and employs a dual-branch architecture to extract complementary local spatial–spectral features from individual spectral groups and the full spectrum. MGA integrates three components: a multi-granularity aggregator (Agg), a spatial–spectral dual-stream attention mechanism (S2-DSA), and a learnable mixed top-k attention mechanism (LMTA). The Agg preserves a token-level pathway while constructing group-proxy pathways with progressively larger local receptive fields, enabling attention to integrate fine-grained information and multi-scale context within a single block. The S2-DSA captures spatial and spectral dependencies through dedicated spatial and spectral attention streams. Furthermore, the LMTA constructs four rank-guided attention branches with different top-k selection ratios and adaptively fuses their outputs to integrate complementary attention responses at varying levels of sparsity. Experiments on three benchmark datasets demonstrate that the MGWT outperforms state-of-the-art methods in HSI classification.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Volumetric radar nowcasting requires models to represent storm evolution across time and altitude without incurring prohibitive computational cost. Conventional two-dimensional approaches omit explicit vertical structure, whereas many three-dimensional models rely on computationally expensive volumetric operators. Reflectivity-only forecasting further fails to exploit complementary information from auxiliary radar variables and terrain. We therefore propose TerraCast3D, an end-to-end framework for deterministic gridded volumetric radar nowcasting conditioned on three polarimetric variables, namely differential reflectivity ZDR, specific differential phase KDP, and copolar correlation coefficient ρHV, together with Doppler spectrum width σV and terrain. TerraCast3D combines source-specific altitude-grouped encoding, terrain-modulated gated fusion, terrain-conditioned modulation of low-altitude reconstruction features, and lightweight spatiotemporal modeling to predict future reflectivity volumes while limiting the use of dense 3D operations. Experiments using GridRad-Severe volumetric radar data from 2019 to 2023 show that TerraCast3D achieves the highest CSI and HSS among the evaluated 3D models at all examined reflectivity thresholds while requiring substantially fewer FLOPs. At 40 dBZ, its full-volume CSI and HSS reach 0.3044 and 0.4501, respectively. Ablation experiments show that auxiliary radar variables mainly improve full-volume continuous-field accuracy and categorical skill at 20 and 30 dBZ, whereas joint auxiliary radar and terrain conditioning yields the highest 40 dBZ and threshold-averaged categorical scores. These findings show that altitude-aware representation and adaptive multi-source conditioning can strengthen intense-echo prediction while maintaining low arithmetic complexity in volumetric radar nowcasting.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reliable through-wall human imaging is challenging because strong wall clutter and motion-dependent target responses can degrade target preservation, particularly under low signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) conditions. This paper proposes a motion-adaptive through-wall moving-target enhancement framework based on energy-guided competitive fusion. After wall clutter suppression and wall-compensated imaging, each pixelwise complex slow-time sequence is decomposed into complementary zero-Doppler and nonzero-Doppler response branches. Kapur maximum-entropy segmentation is then used to construct a common candidate support, from which a data-derived three-branch energy reference distribution is obtained for the zero-Doppler, nonzero-Doppler, and virtual-background hypotheses. The resulting reference-weighted Softmax fusion is formulated as the closed-form solution of a Kullback–Leibler (KL)-regularized optimization problem. Simulations and measured through-wall experiments show that the two decomposed branches provide different relative responses to targets with different motion states, while the proposed fusion improves overall target-background separation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual detection of unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) is an essential perception component of urban low-altitude security systems. However, UAV targets in complex urban scenes appear small and exhibit weak visual features. Existing convolutional structures inadequately extract their contours and fine-grained details, and background clutter such as building textures and trees attenuates target feature responses. To address these problems, this paper proposes a lightweight detection model, SA-YOLO (Small-target Adaptive YOLO). A bilateral detail refinement convolution (BDRConv) strengthens target details while preserving primary features through dual-branch collaboration. An adaptive SimAM (ASimAM) attention mechanism modulates attention response strength via a learnable scaling factor. DySample dynamic upsampling achieves sub-pixel-level reconstruction of cross-scale features through content-adaptive sampling offsets. A weighted joint loss based on the normalized Wasserstein distance improves bounding-box regression for small targets. On the DUT-Anti-UAV and the self-constructed C-UAV datasets, SA-YOLO achieves mAP50-95 values of 52.1% and 43.2%, respectively, with only 2.41 M parameters at 171 FPS. Across five training seeds, the mAP50-95 improvement over the baseline is statistically significant on DUT-Anti-UAV, whereas on C-UAV the gain is directionally consistent but not statistically significant. Compared with the accuracy-leading detectors, SA-YOLO delivers comparable accuracy in a more compact model, achieving a favorable accuracy–model-size tradeoff at a real-time inference speed.

Abstract (英文): Marine oil spill accidents pose serious threats to marine ecosystems and coastal economic development. X-band marine radar provides an effective means for nearshore oil spill monitoring, yet accurate oil film segmentation remains challenging because of grayscale overlap between oil films and sea clutter, range-dependent echo attenuation, and noise interference. To address these challenges, an unsupervised oil spill detection method integrating HBA-AE ROI extraction with IAEA fine segmentation is proposed. In the coarse detection stage, an autoencoder (AE) maps pixel grayscale intensity, local mean, and local standard deviation into a compact two-dimensional latent space. The AE-derived latent representation is further optimized by HBA to enhance oil film–sea clutter separability, after which K-Means clustering identifies candidate oil spill ROIs. In the subsequent fine segmentation stage, the extracted ROI constrains the search domain, while the Improved Alpha Evolution Algorithm (IAEA) maximizes the three-class Otsu between-class variance to determine the optimal dual thresholds for oil film delineation. Experiments using X-band marine radar data collected during the Dalian 7·16 oil spill accident were conducted against seven representative methods. The proposed framework achieved a Precision of 97.20%, an F1-score of 86.60%, and an IoU of 76.40%, demonstrating superior oil film segmentation accuracy and effective false-alarm suppression under complex sea-clutter conditions. It also maintained a favorable balance between segmentation accuracy and computational efficiency, demonstrating its potential for marine oil spill monitoring under the investigated radar observation conditions.

Abstract (英文): The expertly rectified extraction of cropland parcel boundaries is a critical basis for precision agriculture deployment and land resource management. However, the scarcity of large-scale, high-quality, cross-regional reference datasets limits the generalizability of deep learning models. To address this data bottleneck, we created a standardized, multi-source cropland parcel boundary benchmark dataset. The collection, which covers Europe (Denmark, the Netherlands, Spain) and Asia (Central China), encompasses over 180,000 expert-rectified parcels, with average parcel sizes ranging from 0.05 to 10 hectares and spatial resolutions spanning 0.2 to 10 m. Based on this dataset, we evaluated nine representative deep learning models belonging to three architectural paradigms (edge detection, multi-task semantic segmentation, and instance segmentation) and analyzed the effects of spatial resolution, parcel scale, and topographic relief on extraction accuracy. The experimental results indicate the following: (1) Spatial resolution exerts a non-linear influence on boundary extraction fidelity, where some medium-resolution data conditions (such as 4.8 m imagery in large-scale fields) retained competitive boundary extraction performance for certain models. (2) Even small but regular parcels of merely 0.22 hectares can be extracted with high accuracy, achieving a maximum F1 score of 86.58%. (3) Topographic variations lead to contrasting model performance. In plain and hilly terrain, multi-task semantic segmentation models exhibit obvious advantages. BsiNet yields the highest F1 score of 86.58%, but this value drops to 36.56% in complex mountainous regions. By comparison, edge detection models perform better in mountainous areas, where RCF attains the maximum F1 of 70.85%. In summary, this study establishes a standardized, expertly refined reference parcel dataset and provides an empirical evaluation of how different model architectures perform across varied observational settings, offering an empirical baseline for future model development and evaluation in cropland boundary extraction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In general object detection tasks, accurate responses to the boundaries and classifications of potential objects are required of detectors. Given the diverse shapes of objects, these bounding boxes are typically represented as horizontal rectangles. However, in aerial imagery scenarios, horizontal bounding boxes can easily miss valid detections when objects are densely distributed. In recent years, oriented object detection algorithms have achieved considerable success. However, they focus on the boundaries of the object without taking into account its head–tail attributes and true orientation. Getting the true orientation of objects serves as the long-term aim of remote sensing object detection task. To resolve these issues, we decompose the single Gaussian distribution probability loss into a sequential dual-probability distribution, thereby establishing a one-to-one collaborative loss mechanism. This enables the model to learn the real orientation relationship from the dual distribution loss, achieving the acquisition of objects’ true orientation information. To more accurately reflect the preference of detectors, we design a new evaluation metric based on the cosine function and average precision. We conduct a series of experiments on the DOTA dataset, HRSC dataset, and FAIR1M dataset, achieving satisfactory results that validate the effectiveness of the proposed method.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-AOD-based visibility estimation has largely focused on daily-scale estimates or limited overpass times, while its diurnal variations are less understood and discussed. This study therefore investigates surface visibility estimations over China during daytime at 00, 03, 06, and 09 UTC (i.e., 08:00, 11:00, 14:00, and 17:00 China Standard Time, UTC+8), using aerosol optical depth (AOD) observations from the geostationary FY-4B satellite and ERA5 meteorological data. A physically informed framework combines a first-order physical visibility estimate based on AOD, an empirically calibrated relative humidity (RH) adjustment, and XGBoost residual learning. The complete framework, evaluated against ground-based NCEI visibility observations, achieved R = 0.84, RMSE = 2.80 km, and MAE = 2.28 km under sample-based five-fold cross-validation. Independent validation from July 2024 to June 2025 yielded R = 0.79, RMSE = 3.10 km, and MAE = 2.56 km. Visibility-estimation accuracy varied across daytime observation times, with relatively lower errors at 00 and 03 UTC and the largest errors at 06 UTC in both the first-order physical estimate and the final model. Although the complete framework yielded RMSE values of 2.63–3.68 km across the four time slots, these diurnal differences remained evident. The physical estimate also exhibited RH-dependent bias, overestimating visibility at lower RH and underestimating it at higher RH, with a transition near 50%. An empirical RH-conditioned adjustment further reduced estimation errors. These findings highlight the importance of accounting for diurnal and RH-dependent errors in simplified AOD-to-visibility conversion and demonstrate the potential of repeated geostationary observations for daytime surface visibility estimation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In-season crop identification requires accurate, timely, and reliable predictions from asynchronous SAR and optical observations that accumulate progressively during early and middle calendar checkpoints. This study proposes a progressive SAR–optical fusion framework with selective risk control for parcel-level crop monitoring. A Gated Cross-Attention Temporal Network (GCAT) integrates complementary Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 time-series information, while random-prefix training and real day-of-year encoding accommodate asynchronous and unequal-length observations. Probability calibration and a sequential ACCEPT/DEFER strategy further allow uncertain predictions to be deferred until a predefined acceptance criterion is satisfied. Experiments on PASTIS-R show that GCAT achieves a full-season Macro-F1 of 80.57 ± 0.83% across five official folds, outperforming unimodal and multimodal fusion baselines. In the primary Fold-2 evaluation, GCAT provides larger gains than the Sentinel-2-only model at early- and mid-season operational checkpoints, reaches acceptance 7.55 days earlier on average among jointly accepted parcels, and increases accepted coverage. Cross-year evaluation on Brandenburg shows that the classification and earliness advantages of multimodal fusion are partially retained, whereas the confidence–risk relationship degrades markedly. These results highlight the value of SAR–optical fusion for earlier in-season decisions while emphasizing the need for renewed calibration and reliability assessment across years.

Abstract (英文): Assimilating satellite-retrieved profiles is an important approach to improving a numerical model’s initial conditions and typhoon forecasting skill. This study uses temperature and humidity retrieved profile products from the Geostationary Interferometric Infrared Sounder (GIIRS) onboard Fengyun-4B for three typhoons that affected Zhejiang Province in July 2025 with different track types. Sensitivity experiments are conducted under clear-sky, cloudy-sky, and all-sky conditions. Vortex relocation experiments are designed to separate the contributions of typhoon vortex structure and environmental field to track forecasts. Results show that assimilation systematically adjusts temperature and humidity in the typhoon inner core, rainband, and outer environment. All schemes produce lower–middle tropospheric warming and upper tropospheric cooling. Humidity decreases in the low-level outer environment but increases around the typhoon vortex and along the Western Pacific Subtropical High edge. For the three typhoon cases examined in this study, all-sky assimilation is optimal for track forecasts, whereas clear-sky or cloudy-sky assimilation alone can be adverse. For the 24 h accumulated precipitation during the period of severe typhoon impact on Zhejiang, all-sky assimilation increases the threat scores for heavy rain and torrential rain by 0.04 and 0.09, respectively, relative to the control experiment, and increases the extreme torrential rain threat score by 0.09 relative to cloudy-sky assimilation. For the three typhoon cases examined in this study, all-sky GIIRS profile assimilation shows clear advantages for typhoon forecasting and highlights the potential for the operational application of GIIRS-retrieved temperature and humidity profiles.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-altitude lakes on the Tibetan Plateau (TP) are important components of regional water-cycle regulation and climate responses, yet their long-term monitoring remains challenging due to limited in situ observations and sparse spatial sampling of conventional satellite altimetry. The Surface Water and Ocean Topography (SWOT) mission provides two-dimensional observations of inland water-surface elevation (WSE), offering new opportunities for lake monitoring in remote regions. This study evaluates the applicability of SWOT L2 Pixel Cloud (PIXC) observations for TP lake monitoring through cross-sensor comparison with ICESat-2 ATL13 observations. A total of 50 representative TP lakes were analyzed using quality-controlled SWOT–ICESat-2 matched observations. The results demonstrate that SWOT PIXC and ICESat-2 ATL13 generally capture comparable lake-scale WSE variations across the TP. The mean MAE and RMSE were 0.164 m and 0.231 m, respectively, indicating decimeter-scale cross-sensor consistency for most investigated lakes. Lake area showed no significant association with the RMSE, suggesting that lake size alone does not explain the variability of cross-sensor residuals. Further analysis reveals that residual differences are strongly scene-dependent and reflect combined influences from satellite retrieval characteristics, spatial sampling differences, environmental conditions, and temporal offsets rather than SWOT-only uncertainty. These findings provide an independent assessment of SWOT observations over high-altitude lakes and demonstrate their potential for spatially continuous monitoring of TP lake dynamics. The results also establish a foundation for integrating SWOT with multi-source satellite observations to reconstruct long-term lake water-level and storage variations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fine-crack segmentation in high-resolution UAV images of bridges is hindered by weak crack signals, complex backgrounds, and substantial computational demands. This study proposes a dual-stage framework combining lightweight grid-wise screening with graph-enhanced segmentation. CrackClsNet identifies candidate regions, and CrackSegNet processes contextual patches comprising each candidate and its eight neighbors. Multiscale and deformable convolutions, attention, and cross-patch graph reasoning support feature extraction, while overlapping predictions are fused during reconstruction. In experiments using 1000 pixel-annotated crack-containing images collected from 13 highway bridges and 500 crack-free images, neighborhood retention increased candidate region crack-pixel coverage from 91.97% to 95.65% on the classification test set. The end-to-end framework achieved an F1-score of 0.5826, Crack IoU of 0.4110, and mIoU of 0.7052, exceeding six baseline methods in these metrics. The results of ablation experiments further support the benefits of neighboring context and cross-patch feature exchange, with 3 × 3 neighborhoods balancing segmentation quality and computational cost. With approximately 4.41 million parameters, the framework achieved 4.10 FPS with a reported memory consumption of 6.94 GB at 4096 × 5632 pixels, providing the highest throughput and lowest reported memory consumption among the evaluated methods at this resolution. These results indicate a promising balance between segmentation performance and computational cost on the evaluated bridge image dataset.

Abstract (英文): LiDAR–camera fusion for 3D object detection combines the metric geometry of LiDAR measurements with the semantic information in images. However, projected LiDAR depth is sparse and provides incomplete geometric guidance to the image branch, while the modality-specific branches follow different learning dynamics during multimodal training. We present Co-Fusion, a LiDAR-dominant collaborative fusion framework that extends the previously published Co-Fix3D detector. Co-Fusion introduces a LiDAR-depth guidance pipeline that standardizes projected LiDAR depth using dataset-level statistics and encodes the sparse measurements into features aligned with the image branch. A gated fusion network applies multiplicative modulation to the joint image–depth representation to produce depth-aware camera features. A three-stage parameter-transfer training schedule selectively transfers parameters from the LiDAR branch to initialize the camera and fusion branches. Experiments on the nuScenes 3D detection benchmark show that Co-Fusion reaches 74.1% mAP and 75.3% NDS in the single-frame multimodal setting without test-time augmentation.

Abstract (英文): UAV patrol plays a crucial role in combating illegal poppy cultivation. However, the complex and diverse environments in which poppies are cultivated demand effective local and global feature modeling to improve detection performance while keeping computational costs low. Existing CNN–Transformer methods often rely on complex feature interaction to exploit their complementary strengths, and the scarcity of publicly available poppy datasets further restricts the development of poppy detection methods. To address the above issues, we construct an Independent CNN–Transformer Dual-Branch network (ICTNet), which separately models the CNN branch and the Transformer branch, thereby avoiding complex feature interaction. For the Transformer branch, we design a Gated Transformer Block (GTB). GTB employs gated linear attention to model complex spatial information with linear computational complexity, achieving a favorable balance between detection performance and computational efficiency. For the CNN branch, we design a Progressive Convolution Block (PCB) with an expanding receptive field, which effectively models local features while capturing rich contextual information. Moreover, we create a multi-scene poppy detection dataset named MS-Poppy, which contains 2443 images covering six UAV-view scenes. Extensive experiments are conducted on the MS-Poppy and the public GWHD 2021 dataset. Experimental results indicate that, compared with the advanced YOLO12, ICTNet reduces parameters by 11% while improving mAP@0.5 by 5.4% on the MS-Poppy dataset and by 1.0% on the GWHD 2021 dataset, respectively.

Abstract (英文): Urban agglomerations exhibit pronounced ecological heterogeneity due to complex interactions between anthropogenic and natural factors. However, most existing studies assess ecological conditions from an aggregated regional perspective, potentially obscuring localized ecological mechanisms and heterogeneous ecological responses among different functional areas. To address this issue, an integrated ecological assessment framework incorporating anthropogenic indicators was developed to investigate the spatiotemporal heterogeneity and driving mechanisms of ecological conditions across major function-oriented zones in the Beijing–Tianjin–Hebei region from 2001 to 2020. The results revealed significant functional-zone-dependent heterogeneity in ecological responses. The ecological conservation area maintained the highest ecological conditions with continuous improvement, largely attributed to extensive vegetation coverage and ecological restoration efforts. In contrast, the core function area showed relatively low ecological conditions, primarily constrained by urban thermal stress, atmospheric pollution, and intensive human activities. The coastal development area displayed fragmented ecological patterns, with ecological responses influenced jointly by climatic conditions and socioeconomic activities. The secondary function area exhibited more complex ecological dynamics, where ecological conditions were shaped by the interaction of land cover, GDP, and natural factors. These findings demonstrate that ecological responses within urban agglomerations are fundamentally shaped by functional-zone-dependent anthropogenic–natural coupling processes. By integrating anthropogenic indicators into ecological assessment and examining ecological dynamics from a functional-zone perspective, the proposed framework enables the identification of localized ecological mechanisms and heterogeneous driver–response relationships that may be obscured by aggregated regional analyses. Furthermore, the proposed framework provides scientific support for functional-zone-oriented ecological governance, differentiated ecological restoration, and sustainable regional development.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Infrared imagery supports low-light target perception, but acquiring, registering, and annotating paired visible–infrared imagery is costly. Visible-to-infrared image translation can alleviate this demand, yet cross-modal differences often degrade object boundaries and spatial layouts, especially for small targets. We propose DFSMamba to preserve geometric structure throughout visible-to-infrared translation by connecting frequency-guided feature formation, input-dependent directional aggregation, and infrared-domain reconstruction supervision. A discrete wavelet transform (DWT)-based detail-guided module supplements bottleneck features with learned structural cues, direction-adaptive 2D selective scan adjusts the fusion of aligned directional responses, and state-space similarity matching constrains the output in a fixed multiscale feature space learned through real-infrared reconstruction. Experiments on FMB, VEDAI, and AVIID-1 include image-quality comparisons, target-level evaluation, ablation studies, efficiency analysis, and downstream vehicle detection. Among evaluated methods, DFSMamba achieves the best SSIM, MS-SSIM, PSNR, and L1 values on all three datasets. On AVIID-1, it obtains an SSIM of 0.985 and a PSNR of 41.799 dB. A YOLOv9-C detector trained on generated images reaches an AP50:95 of 0.636 on real infrared test images, compared with 0.640 when trained on real infrared images. These results show that DFSMamba preserves detection-relevant structure and supports the use of generated images as synthetic training data in the evaluated aerial vehicle-detection setting.

Abstract (英文): The inherent class imbalance and scarcity of labeled samples remain major challenges in hyperspectral image (HSI) classification. SMOTE-based methods alleviate class imbalance by generating synthetic minority-class samples. However, their interpolation mechanisms often fail to capture spatial–spectral features adequately, resulting in noisy or boundary-ambiguous samples. To address this issue, this paper proposes Spatial–Spectral Prototype-guided Deep SMOTE (SSPD) for imbalanced HSI classification. Firstly, SSPD constructs a spatial–spectral representation guided by feature importance to suppress redundant spectral bands and isolated spatial noise. Secondly, SSPD employs a prototype-guided oversampling strategy to generate synthetic minority-class samples. Class prototypes determine the generation direction, while feature-importance weights constrain the interpolation process to preserve discriminative spectral responses. Finally, a stepwise ensemble with soft voting is employed to integrate classifiers trained under different imbalance ratios. Extensive experiments were conducted on Indian Pines, Pavia University, Salinas, and WHU-Hi benchmark datasets. Compared with the individual machine-learning classifiers, SSPD achieves more stable and competitive performance in terms of Overall Accuracy (OA), Average Accuracy (AA), and Kappa Coefficient (KC).

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-term satellite observations of cloud amount provide critical constraints on climate sensitivity and are essential for reducing uncertainties in climate projections. Different cloud types exert distinct radiative effects and exhibit divergent responses to warming. Yet, their relative contributions to trends in total cloud amount remain inadequately quantified. This study analyzes 17 years (2003–2019) of MODIS observations and reveals that, although global and zonal (60°S–60°N) mean trends in total cloud amount are weak, significant local signals persist. Trend decomposition demonstrates that free-tropospheric cloud occurrence dominates the magnitude of total cloud amount trends, whereas low-level cloud (LowC) size primarily dictates their spatial distribution. Nevertheless, this LowC size signal is substantially suppressed by an opposing LowC occurrence signal, resulting in a significant decoupling between LowC and total cloud amounts. To address the inherent limitations of passive satellite retrievals, we further utilize ERA5 reanalysis data. This data further indicates that approximately half of the trend in LowC occurrence arises from cloud overlap biases unresolved by passive satellite sensors. Correcting for this bias should yield a coherent shift in LowC amount trends over most oceans, suggesting that passive satellite retrievals may have systematically obscured the response of low-level clouds to recent climate change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote-sensing image change captioning (RSICC) generally assumes correctly paired bitemporal images and focuses on isolated sentence descriptions, limiting applicability to archives where the correct temporal counterpart may be unknown. We introduce the Noisy-Reference Paragraph-Level Change-Captioning (NRP-CC) task, which requires retrieving the post-change observation from a noisy candidate pool before generating a coherent multi-statement paragraph. We construct the SECOND-ParCap dataset with image-level semantic annotations and paragraph-level change descriptions, and propose a Weakly Supervised Detection-Guided Historical-Cascaded Network (WSDHC-Net). WSDHC-Net leverages Dual Similarity Matching (DS-Matching) for retrieval from noisy candidate pools and employs weakly supervised detection to mine semantic cues for the Historical-Cascaded Paragraph Refinement Change-Captioning (HCPR-CC) module, which generates multi-statement paragraphs describing scene changes. We further introduce SA-ROUGE to evaluate sentence-level correspondence across the generated statements. On SECOND-ParCap, WSDHC-Net achieves Acc@1 scores of 99.53%, 99.29%, and 98.52% under appearance-hard retrieval with 5, 10, and 20 candidates, respectively. In a separate captioning benchmark using identical frozen retrieved inputs across all compared methods, WSDHC-Net attains ROUGE-L and SA-ROUGE scores of 0.5017 and 0.5016, respectively, under reference-assisted sentence-level evaluation; the corresponding paragraph-level scores are 0.2749 and 1.9305. These results support the feasibility of integrating temporal-correspondence retrieval with paragraph-level change description in NRP-CC.

Abstract (英文): Ma bamboo (Dendrocalamus latiflorus Munro) is an ecologically and economically important sympodial bamboo species widely cultivated in southern China. Mapping its spatial distribution with satellite imagery remains challenging due to spectral response overlaps between Ma bamboo and other forest types. This study assessed the potential of integrating seasonal spectral variation and a targeted SWIR2–RE1 spectral contrast from Sentinel-2 to improve Ma bamboo mapping. Four normalized seasonal metrics were derived from winter and summer observations. A Ma Bamboo Spectral Index (MBSI) was developed based on the normalized contrast between SWIR2 and RE1. Four feature configurations were evaluated using XGBoost and nested spatial cross-validation. SHAP and accumulated local effects were further employed to interpret the model. The results showed that the model integrating annual spectral variables, seasonal metrics, and MBSI performed best, with an overall accuracy of 0.833, an F1 score of 0.819, and an AUC of 0.875. Seasonal metrics primarily improved producer’s accuracy, whereas MBSI contributed more to user’s accuracy, indicating complementary effects on reducing omission and commission errors. MBSI was the most important predictor, while RE1c was the most important seasonal metric and showed the strongest interaction with MBSI. These results show that combining seasonal spectral variation with the SWIR2–RE1 contrast can improve the completeness and reliability of Ma bamboo mapping and provide an interpretable framework for mapping in southern China.

Abstract (英文): Rapid post-event landslide mapping is essential for emergency response and disaster management; however, accurately delineating rainfall-induced landslides remains challenging because of their fragmented morphology, heterogeneous landscapes, and the complementary nature of Earth Observation data. Although recent deep learning approaches have demonstrated promising performance using optical imagery, the contribution of different geo-auxiliary information to landslide segmentation and its transferability across regions remain insufficiently understood. This study proposes a Residual Dual-Branch SegFormer that integrates heterogeneous geospatial information through a lightweight residual Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) encoder and a novel Multi-Scale Geo-Auxiliary Gated Fusion Module (MGFM), enabling adaptive feature fusion while preserving the original SegFormer architecture. Using a multimodal dataset constructed from the March 2023 Tropical Cyclone Freddy event in southern Malawi, we systematically evaluate RGB imagery combined with optical, terrain, and Sentinel-1 SAR-derived auxiliary layers. The framework is assessed on a geographically independent test area and further validated through zero-shot transfer on the public Landslide4Scene and Lushan datasets. Experimental results demonstrate the following: (1) Integrating pre- and post-event SAR coherence with RGB imagery achieves the best overall performance when applied to areas that match the training data, reaching a mean Intersection over Union (mIoU) of 80.56% and an F1-score of 78.03% in the independent Malawi test area. (2) RGB combined with slope represents the most effective among the evaluated static topographic configurations in zero-shot transfer to the two external datasets, consistently outperforming the RGB baseline. (3) The proposed framework exhibits strong cross-region generalization, achieving the highest transfer performance on both external datasets while demonstrating that SAR coherence provides the most discriminative auxiliary information for post-event rainfall-induced landslide segmentation. These findings provide practical guidance for designing robust multimodal deep learning frameworks for operational landslide mapping.

Abstract (英文): Complex rotational motion of a ship induces severe range migration and time-varying Doppler modulation in radar echoes. The time-domain generalized Radon–Fourier transform (TGRFT) can jointly compensate for these effects, but its multidimensional search over the initial range and all motion coefficients leads to a high computational burden. This paper proposes a two-dimensional frequency-domain GRFT (2D-FGRFT) for fast motion-parameter estimation and inverse synthetic aperture radar (ISAR) imaging of ships. The two-dimensional frequency-domain expression of a radar echo with a polynomial slant-range history is first derived, based on which a corresponding frequency-domain matched filter is constructed. The matched filter is parameterized only by the second- and higher-order frequency-domain motion coefficients. By exploiting the structure of the derived expression, a two-dimensional inverse fast Fourier transform (2D-IFFT) is performed after matched filtering, replacing the explicit searches over the initial range and first-order motion coefficient while preserving coherent integration of echoes from multiple scatterers on the ship. Finally, the motion parameters are determined from the matched-filter parameters and the peak locations obtained through constant false alarm rate (CFAR) detection, enabling fast ISAR imaging of the ship. Computer simulations and experimental results validate the computational efficiency, noise robustness, and overall performance of the proposed algorithm.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mountain vegetation change is commonly summarized as a mean greening or browning trend, or as the displacement of a discrete boundary; both can miss changes in the shape of the elevation profile of vegetation greenness. Here we tested whether the greenness profile on the southern flank of the Tianshan Mountains reorganized between 1989 and 2024, using a Landsat transect along the Dushanzi–Kuqa (G217) highway. NDVI and EVI2 profiles were constructed over a fixed 1000–4100 m elevation domain using only sampling locations valid in all comparison periods; profiles were mean-centered, and the early-to-recent contrast was tested with spatial-block permutations across 36 combinations of temporal window, corridor width, elevation-bin width, and index. All 36 combinations showed significant shape change (p ≤ 0.005) with cross-scenario concordance, and the contrast persisted after adjustment for cross-sensor differences. The contrast followed a consistent three-band structure, with relative decreases at 1100–1950 m, increases at 2550–3300 m, and renewed decreases at 3550–4100 m. Absolute greenness increased almost everywhere (more than 97% of 25,334 samples), but the increase was four to five times larger in the mid-elevation band than in the lower and upper bands, varied eightfold along the transect, and was organized primarily by elevation and along-transect position (spatially cross-validated random-forest R2 ≈ 0.66). The vegetation contrast was not directionally consistent with decadal temperature, soil-moisture, or snow contrasts. Greenness change along this transect was therefore an elevation-dependent redistribution of the whole profile rather than uniform greening or a single boundary displacement, and no robust directional correspondence with decadal hydroclimate contrasts was detected under the present decadal analysis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate landslide susceptibility assessment (LSA) is essential for regional disaster prevention and mitigation planning. Although machine learning and deep learning models have been widely adopted for LSA, their transferability across regions is often compromised by distributional discrepancies between source and target datasets. To address this challenge, we adapted the conditional domain adversarial network (CDAN) framework for cross-regional landslide susceptibility assessment to mitigate domain shift and improve cross-regional robustness. Two cross-regional transfer experiments were conducted to compare CDAN with conventional machine learning, direct-transfer deep learning, and representative domain adaptation methods. The results showed that CDAN achieved the highest mean Accuracy and F1 score among the benchmark models in both target domains, with Accuracy values of 0.77 and 0.80 and F1 scores of 0.78 and 0.80, respectively. The corresponding values of the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) were 0.837 and 0.836, respectively. Neighborhood-based spatial information also improved the direct-transfer performance of conventional machine learning models compared with single-pixel representations. These results indicate that the CDAN-based framework can reduce the reliance on labeled target-domain samples and support county-level cross-regional landslide susceptibility assessment under domain shift.

</details>

A daily first frontal analysis south of Hainan showed reductions of 15.3% in mean SST gradient and 25.6% in the mean daily strong front area between paired 15-day windows. Photosynthetically available radiation decreased by 24.1%, and diffuse attenuation at 490 nm increased by 7.9% across their respective windows. Water-change masks identified 126.5 km2 of potential new inundation candidates, including 1.5 km2 of same-pixel multisensor overlap. The analysis documents marine changes, prolonged rainfall and regional water-change candidates within their stated observation windows.

Abstract (英文): The difficulty in accurately determining channel Manning’s roughness has long constrained the accuracy of river flood simulation. At present, Manning’s roughness is mainly assigned using empirical approaches such as lookup tables and visual comparison, which are insufficient to represent the spatial heterogeneity of roughness in natural rivers. Remote sensing provides valuable data support for flood simulation in areas with sparse hydrological gauges. Remote-sensing observations are objective and can be acquired over large areas, but they are temporally discontinuous, whereas hydrodynamic models provide physically meaningful and temporally continuous simulations. These two information sources are therefore naturally complementary, providing a new avenue for Manning’s roughness inversion through remote sensing and hydrodynamic model coupling. To address these challenges, this study proposes a new remote sensing and hydrodynamic model coupling framework centered on roughness inversion. SAR-derived inundation extent is used as a spatial constraint, and an objective function together with particle swarm optimization (PSO) drives an iterative closed loop between the one-dimensional HEC-RAS model and the remote-sensing constraint to autonomously approach the optimal roughness parameters and improve the reliability of flood simulation. To bridge the scale mismatch between one-dimensional hydrodynamic outputs and two-dimensional SAR observations, the HEC-RAS cross-section geometry TIN structure is used to spatialize the simulated water surface, enabling direct comparison between simulated and SAR-observed inundation extents. The Le’an River in Wuyuan, Jiangxi Province, China, was selected as the test reach to evaluate the applicability and reliability of the framework under sparse-gauge conditions. The model was calibrated using the SAR-derived inundation extent, and simulated water levels were subsequently compared with rating-curve-derived reference levels. Under the SAR inundation constraint, the agreement between simulated and observed inundation extents improved, with IoU increasing from 0.763 under the initial parameter set to 0.789. Comparison with rating-curve-derived reference water levels further showed that the optimized Manning’s roughness parameters improved water-level simulation, reducing RMSE from 0.811 m to 0.594 m and increasing NSE from 0.950 to 0.973. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method when continuous hydrological observations are unavailable. The framework provides an automated remote-sensing-constrained approach for Manning’s roughness inversion in hydrodynamic models of small and medium-sized rivers with limited in situ hydrological data.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate hourly regional precipitation forecasting is essential for flood warning and weather-risk management, but station-scale global navigation satellite system (GNSS) precipitation models cannot fully describe the spatial evolution of rainfall between stations. This study proposes an hourly regional precipitation forecasting (HRPF) model that combines gridded GNSS-derived precipitable water vapor (PWV) and gridded precipitation with a convolutional long short-term memory (ConvLSTM) encoder–decoder network. Missing values in the hourly PWV and precipitation records from 69 GNSS-collocated meteorological stations in Taiwan Province during 2008–2012 were reconstructed using singular spectrum analysis and then resampled onto a 0.06° latitude–longitude grid using nearest-neighbor interpolation. The model uses PWV and precipitation fields from the preceding 1–6 h as two input channels and predicts the precipitation field one hour ahead. Lagged autocorrelation analysis showed strong spatiotemporal dependence in both precipitation and PWV fields, with coefficients above 0.86 and 0.85, respectively. In annual-scale external validation, the HRPF model maintained probability of detection (POD) values above 90%, false alarm rate (FAR) values below 55%, and threat score (TS) values of 46.22–46.58%. Seasonal external validation showed POD values of 88.8–97.8%, with the highest TS in summer (52.8–53.3%) owing to lower FAR values (45.8–46.4%). These results indicate that GNSS-derived PWV can provide useful spatial water-vapor information for one-hour-ahead regional precipitation forecasting, while model skill remains season-dependent over Taiwan’s complex terrain.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reliable drought assessment requires spatially continuous drought information, whereas station-based drought indices are spatially discrete and many existing gridded products are available at relatively coarse spatial scales. To address this issue, this study generates a monthly Geographically Weighted Composite Drought Index, termed GCDI-1, on a 1-km output grid over China. A geographically weighted SPEI-related auxiliary variable, termed GeoSPEI-1, is constructed from neighboring station-based SPEI-1 values to incorporate neighboring-station drought information into drought index generation. GeoSPEI-1 is jointly used with multi-source remote sensing and reanalysis variables as input to a geographically weighted feature-learning model, termed GeoCSL, which combines convolutional neural network, self-attention, and long short-term memory components to process the multi-source inputs. Input-combination experiments showed that GeoSPEI-1 was the primary contributor among the evaluated input variables within GeoCSL, while the multi-source environmental variables provided additional complementary information. Ten-fold cross-validation using the 2001–2010 training samples yielded an RMSE of 0.546 and an MAE of 0.407 for GeoCSL against station-based SPEI-1. Station-referenced evaluation using the temporally held-out 2011–2012 data further showed that GCDI-1 achieved an RMSE of 0.556 and an MAE of 0.410, compared with 0.562 and 0.421 for leave-one-station-out Kriging and 0.754 and 0.577 for SPEIbase-1, respectively. These results indicate that incorporating geographically weighted neighboring-station information together with multi-source environmental variables can support the generation of spatially continuous drought information and the use of GCDI-1 as a supplementary gridded product for regional drought monitoring and assessment in China.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study evaluates the accuracy and usability of two low-cost uncrewed aerial system (UAS) multispectral sensors—MicaSense RedEdge-M and SlantRange 3p—using a dual-scale comparison framework. Calibrated 3% and 48% reflectance tarps and Analytical Spectral Device (ASD) field spectrometry were used for point-scale absolute validation. The NEON imaging spectrometer (NIS), a well-calibrated airborne hyperspectral sensor, was utilized for a field-scale comparison by spectrally resampling the hyperspectral data to align with the multispectral bands and performing spatial co-registration and aggregation of the multispectral data to a common 1 m grid. Over the 48% tarp, MicaSense Near-infrared (NIR) reflectance was 7.2% below the ASD reference while SlantRange NIR was 7.9% lower, validating previous studies that have observed the underestimation of the NIR band with UAS multispectral sensors. Both sensors overestimated the 3% tarp across all bands when compared to the ASD measurements. Field-scale regressions yielded R2 values above 0.90 for most bands, while NIR slopes were 0.74 for MicaSense and 0.72 for SlantRange. Consistent with these trends, the largest radiometric errors occurred in the NIR region, where both sensors increasingly underestimated high-reflectance surfaces relative to NIS. Median Normalized Difference Vegetation Index (NDVI) differences relative to NIS were −0.027 and −0.064, and these became more negative with an increasing NIS NDVI. Edge buffering reduced MicaSense bias, whereas the SlantRange showed limited edge effects. These results demonstrate that low-cost UAS multispectral imagery can support ecological applications when calibration, reflectance range, view geometry, and ecosystem-specific validation requirements are explicitly considered.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deformation of glacier–bedrock systems provides evidence of slope adjustment before catastrophic failure. In a post-event assessment, we investigate the 26 August 2026 Langtang rock–ice failure in Nepal using NASA-ISRO Synthetic Aperture Radar (NISAR) observations to relate pre-event displacement to rock–ice interaction and valley confinement. Seven ascending line-of-sight (LOS) intervals characterize temporal change, while the final pre-event ascending–descending pair (P4) provides east–west and vertical displacement estimates. P4 spans 24-day windows ending on 19 and 16 August 2026. Relative to the surrounding terrain, median eastward and vertical displacements are +24.85 and −19.03 mm within the sampled possible detachment mapped by the United Nations Satellite Centre (UNOSAT), compared with +41.05 and +33.12 mm in the neighboring LOS deformation area. Opposing vertical tendencies and local east–west contrasts indicate differential movement across the rock–ice system. A positive LOS cluster lies 1.393 km from the possible detachment boundary and retains 78.8% of its connected area at coherence ≥ 0.70. The ascending record shows a positive pre-event contrast, a negative event-spanning contrast and a mixed post-event field. Together, these observations underpin a geological model in which oblique sliding increases loading at neighboring ice–bedrock contacts, promotes uplift and westward deflection, and transfers loading toward a valley constriction. We interpret this sequence as south to north, counter-clockwise adjustment accompanying differential sliding. The model connects the measured deformation pattern to structural weakness, changing glacial support and topographic confinement, providing a geological explanation for pre-event rock–ice adjustment within the source to valley hazard cascade.

</details>

Abstract (英文): THz-SAR provides high-resolution imaging but is highly sensitive to trajectory perturbations, while video SAR further requires efficient reconstruction of consecutive sub-aperture frames from incomplete measurements. Under variable curvature motion, forward-model errors introduce geometric and phase inconsistencies that weaken the interframe correlation exploited by tensor recovery. This article develops an integrated radius-of-curvature change rate (RCRC)-guided low-tubal-rank recovery framework for sparse aperture THz video SAR. An RCRC-aware variable curvature trajectory operator models deterministic motion-dependent effects, a tensor nuclear-norm prior exploits interframe redundancy, and pulsewise unit modulus phase factors compensate for residual perturbations. The resulting joint problem is solved by alternating proximal-gradient, tensor singular value thresholding, and closed-form phase updates. Controlled point target simulations and measured data experiments are conducted at pulse retention ratios of 60%, 40%, and 30%. A single global slow-time mask is applied to the shared pulse stream before sub-aperture slicing, ensuring consistent pulse omissions across overlapping frames. Comparisons with three literature baselines evaluate the integrated method, while two internal ablations examine the effects of RCRC modeling and residual phase estimation within the adopted TNN-regularized formulation. The 40% case preserves the principal scattering structure and is treated as a candidate sparse observation operating point, whereas the degradation at 30% identifies a more severe robustness boundary. Because the sparse observations are generated retrospectively from full-rate measurements, the experiments validate reconstruction under missing pulses but do not demonstrate physical reductions in PRF, transmitted pulses, or collected raw data volume.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Under dense vegetation cover, deep networks for lithological classification must resolve small spectral differences between rock–vegetation mixtures. To address this issue, we propose the Dual-Branch Multi-Source Lithological Classification Network (DBMSL-Net), which uses Gaofen-5 Advanced Hyperspectral Imager (GF-5 AHSI) hyperspectral imagery, Phased Array type L-band Synthetic Aperture Radar-2 (PALSAR-2) data, and terrain data. Specifically, one branch encodes pixel spectra, and the other encodes spatial features from radar and terrain observations in the surrounding neighborhood. The spectral branch retains the overall spectral shape and local responses within intervals derived from absorption features in laboratory rock spectra. Laboratory pretraining initializes this branch, and source-domain class prototypes provide additional spectral references. A reliability gate adjusts the prototype contribution using target-pixel features and their similarity to the prototypes. The two branches are fused using weights that depend on the normalized difference vegetation index and pixel features. The core classifier, the differentiable binning residual module (DBRM), is trained jointly with both branches. It learns piecewise feature–class relationships through soft binning and refines class scores through successive residual updates. During inference, full-spectrum and feature-interval predictions are combined using class-specific weights. Neighborhood adjustment accounts for target extent. Across runs, boundary-aligned minimum-margin consensus is used for the spatially restricted target, whereas probability averaging is used for the extensive target. We evaluate DBMSL-Net in Lannitang, Yunnan, China, where quartz monzonite porphyry and quartz diorite porphyrite have similar mixed-pixel spectra and markedly different labeled sample sizes. On the within-area test subset, DBMSL-Net achieves a Target Macro F1 of 0.8301. Average accuracy, macro-averaged F1, and mean intersection over union reach 0.9388, 0.8780, and 0.7897, respectively. All four metrics exceed those of the nine comparison methods under the shared evaluation framework. Ablation experiments show that neither branch alone approaches the complete system (0.5727 and 0.7135, compared with 0.8301), and that the residual stage adds a measurable gain in both the initial and the complete configuration. The classification maps preserve the distributions of both the local and the extensive target and support lithological discrimination and field verification in this vegetated study area.

Sixteen static environmental factors were combined with three features derived from small baseline subset InSAR (SBAS-InSAR): long-term mean deformation velocity (Vmean), velocity variability (Vstd), and velocity trend (Kv). Adding Vmean yielded modest median paired AUC gains of 0.003–0.005 across the four models under the primary 10 km spatial validation scheme. XGB achieved the highest mean AUC (0.914) with the M1 feature set, comprising the static factors and Vmean. Adding Kv or Vstd did not yield consistent further gains. Out-of-sample Shapley additive explanations (OOS-SHAP) identified rainfall, the coefficient of variation in elevation (CV), distance to faults, and lithology as the leading predictors; Vmean also made an appreciable contribution. The main predictors exhibited nonlinear responses. Spatial SHAP mapping and Moran’s I analysis further revealed pronounced spatial variation and significant clustering in feature contributions. These findings indicate that long-term mean InSAR deformation velocity provides a consistent but modest complement to conventional static factors, primarily through local adjustments to predicted susceptibility and the identification of regional differences.

Abstract (英文): Hybrid radiative transfer model–machine learning (RTM–ML) approaches can incorporate physical constraints into vegetation-trait retrieval, but their advantage over empirical learning remains uncertain for very-high-resolution, limited-band observations of structurally heterogeneous forest canopies. We compared six empirical machine-learning regression algorithms with a hybrid PROSAIL–Gaussian Process Regression (GPR) approach for retrieving derived canopy chlorophyll content (CCC) from four-band UAV multispectral imagery of pedunculate oak (Quercus robur L.) stands under contrasting oak lace bug (Corythucha arcuata) pressure. At the infested site, GPR provided the strongest performance in May (R2 = 0.61, RMSE = 27.04 µg cm−2, NRMSE = 10.87%), whereas Random Forest performed best in September. At the reference site, Random Forest, least-squares linear regression, and kernel ridge regression provided the strongest results depending on sampling period and performance metric, with R2 reaching 0.60. According to the primary cross-validated predictive metrics (R2, RMSE, and NRMSE), the tested PROSAIL–GPR approach generally showed lower predictive performance than the empirical models under the same tree-grouped evaluation framework. Overall, incorporating PROSAIL-simulated information did not improve predictive performance under the tested four-band, crown-scale configuration. Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral anomaly detection (HAD) aims to identify Earth’s surface anomalies without prior knowledge. However, most existing unsupervised HAD methods rely on training data roughly partitioned into background and anomalies, suffering from severe class imbalance and yielding feature representations heavily tied to scene-specific distributions. In contrast, supervised hyperspectral classification networks can extract well-organized spectral–spatial representations from diverse land-cover types; yet, this valuable knowledge remains underutilized in HAD due to the absence of labeled anomaly samples. To circumvent this limitation, this paper proposes a novel knowledge transfer paradigm from classification tasks to anomaly detection. The proposed framework employs a pretrained convolutional enhanced Transformer (CET) classification network as the teacher model, transferring its rich spectral–spatial context encodings via feature-level distillation. Concurrently, by synergizing latent feature alignment, Gaussian mixture model (GMM) priors, and reconstruction objectives, the student network is equipped with compact background modeling and highly discriminative representation abilities, which provide robust support for feature-space anomaly detection. Extensive experiments on spaceborne and airborne hyperspectral datasets demonstrate that our method substantially improves detection accuracy against complex terrestrial backgrounds with fast inference, while exhibiting superior cross-scene generalization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Small-object detection in UAV imagery remains challenging because repeated downsampling weakens fine structural cues, conventional feature pyramids are poorly aligned with small-object-dominated scale distributions, and fixed detection heads provide limited adaptation. To address these issues, a Hierarchical–Bidirectional–Dynamic Network (HBD-Net) is developed as a parameter-compact detector based on YOLOv11s. HEEM reinforces edge, contour, and texture information at multiple backbone stages using orthogonal depthwise strip convolutions, residual detail extraction, and zero-initialized fusion. PLSS replaces the conventional P3–P5 hierarchy with P2–P4 to emphasize high-resolution features, while P2-BiFPN introduces bidirectional propagation and learnable weighted fusion. An integrated Dynamic Head further refines features across scale, spatial, and task dimensions. On VisDrone, HBD-Net achieves 39.0% mAP50 and 22.6% mAP50–95, improving the YOLOv11s baseline by 6.9 and 4.3 percentage points, respectively, while reducing parameters from 9.4 M to 3.7 M. Ablation studies confirm the complementary effects of the components. On HIT-UAV, HBD-Net reaches 84.1% mAP50 and 57.0% mAP50–95, indicating effectiveness under infrared aerial imaging. These results demonstrate that coordinated structural enhancement, high-resolution multiscale fusion, and adaptive prediction can improve UAV small-object detection with a compact parameter footprint.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate aircraft-region segmentation in aerial infrared imagery can provide a useful scene-understanding component for image-assisted aviation inspection. However, target scale variation and ambiguous boundaries remain challenging in simulated aerial infrared scenes.
