Abstract (英文): Squinted multi-channel spaceborne synthetic aperture radar (SAR) offers wide-swath coverage and flexible observation geometry. However, imaging algorithms based on chirp scaling (CS) may broaden the range spectrum beyond the available sampling bandwidth, causing range spectrum aliasing and degrading the imaging quality of targets at the range edges of wide-swath scenes. To address this problem, this paper proposes a range-subband imaging method. First, a range spectrum broadening model is established to identify the main factors affecting spectral broadening. A subband division criterion is then derived to determine the minimum number of subbands required to avoid aliasing. Finally, a processing procedure comprising range-subband division, individual subband focusing, and coherent fusion is developed to recover the full-band range-focused signal. Point-target and distributed-target simulations verify that the proposed method suppresses range-spectrum aliasing and preserves full-band range resolution, while a broadside airborne experiment confirms that it introduces no appreciable image-quality degradation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Emerging remote sensing technologies increasingly support coastal observation through Earth observation (EO) derivatives such as satellite-derived shorelines, unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)- and light detection and ranging (LiDAR)-based elevation models, coastal land-use/land-cover products, bathymetric grids, turbidity and chlorophyll-a indicators, habitat maps, flood extents, and artificial intelligence (AI)-generated segmentation outputs. These EO-derived products are central to shoreline monitoring, coastal vulnerability assessment, flood-response planning, marine ecosystem management, and land–sea administration. Yet their evidential value depends on transparent provenance: users must know which sensor, acquisition condition, preprocessing chain, algorithm, model version, validation dataset, and uncertainty estimate produced each coastal product. The review synthesises 156 eligible publications identified through a systematic review process. Of these, 89 studies containing sufficient implementation and methodological detail were selected for detailed analytical coding. Following the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses (PRISMA) protocol, the review analyses application domains, provenance capture modes, representation strategies, semantic web technologies, and interoperability practices. A coastal EO thematic refinement translates the review findings into provenance requirements for satellite, UAV, LiDAR, Internet of Things (IoT), open-water, and AI-enabled coastal observation workflows. The review identifies persistent gaps in feature-level lineage, alignment between the World Wide Web Consortium Provenance Ontology (PROV-O) and International Organization for Standardization (ISO) geospatial metadata standards, spatial semantics, AI workflow transparency, uncertainty traceability, and scalable querying. To address these limitations, the paper introduces GeoPROV, a lightweight semantic profile of PROV-O designed for spatially explicit, feature-level provenance. GeoPROV aligns PROV-O with ISO 19115/19157, GeoSPARQL, Shapes Constraint Language (SHACL), and SpatioTemporal Asset Catalogue (STAC)-compatible catalogue practices. The framework enables EO-derived coastal products to be traced from raw observation through preprocessing, AI/geoprocessing, validation, catalogue publication, and coastal-administrative decision-making. The resulting GeoPROV profile integrates PROV-O, GeoSPARQL, SHACL, ISO 19115/19157 lineage concepts, and STAC metadata within a lightweight semantic framework demonstrated through a coastal EO product workflow and machine-actionable provenance implementation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ecological changes in mining areas are characterized by long-term dynamics, temporal stages, and spatial heterogeneity. Existing monitoring methods often face challenges in balancing multidimensional ecological condition characterization with spatially consistent analysis and have limited capability to identify complex disturbance–recovery trajectories. To address these issues, an Object-based Ecological Index-driven Framework (OEI-GTCD) is proposed to monitor ecological disturbance and recovery trajectories in mining areas. The framework uses the Mining-area Ecological Index (MEI) to characterize multidimensional ecological conditions and conducts analysis based on spatially consistent object units. On this basis, a Globally Optimized Time-series Change Detection method (GTCD) is proposed to improve the identification of complex ecological disturbance–recovery trajectories by integrating trend-change detection, abrupt-change detection, and segmentation optimization. Landsat imagery from 2000 to 2024 was used to characterize the spatiotemporal patterns of ecological disturbance and recovery across the Daye Mining Area, China. The findings suggest that MEI captures multiple dimensions of ecological conditions, while object-scale analysis characterizes ecological changes through overall object-level trajectories, supporting their spatial interpretation. OEI-GTCD achieved disturbance and recovery detection accuracies of 88.4% and 84.8%, respectively, representing improvements of 6.0 and 5.2 percentage points over OEI-LandTrendr. Ablation analyses further highlighted the contributions of abrupt-change detection and the improved segmentation criterion to event identification, as well as the role of local breakpoint refinement in temporal localization. From 2000 to 2024, stable and continuously improving ecological states together accounted for 82.07% of the area represented by the non-water objects used in the ecological analysis, while disturbance and recovery processes exhibited pronounced spatiotemporal heterogeneity. Overall, OEI-GTCD provides an integrated approach for monitoring ecological disturbance and recovery and characterizing their spatiotemporal patterns in mining areas.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The accuracy of 3D digitisation methods is strongly influenced by the surface properties of the observed object, particularly its texture, reflectance, and material structure. This is especially relevant to the systematic metrological analysis of ancient stone vessels undertaken by the Artifact Research Foundation, where high-resolution 3D models are increasingly used for geometric analysis, including through dedicated tools such as Petrie Scope. This study assesses the potential influence of crystalline granite texture on residual dispersion in Structure-from-Motion (SfM) photogrammetry and handheld laser-triangulation scanning using granite metrological reference objects measured under controlled conditions before and after application of a self-sublimating scanning spray. For the unsprayed cube, the pooled empirical 99% residual interval was −33.8 to +33.1 µm for photogrammetry and −49.9 to +54.5 µm for handheld scanning; for the unsprayed cylinder, the corresponding intervals were −51.6 to +30.7 µm and −76.3 to +55.3 µm. Sprayed reconstructions in the principal comparison exhibited lower residual dispersion and circularity RMSD. Repeated handheld-scanner acquisitions supported this trend, while supplementary SfM analyses demonstrated the influence of camera-network configuration and calibration treatment on surface-residual dispersion. By linking surface residuals to circularity and centre-offset descriptors, this study provides an empirical basis for interpreting geometric regularity in ancient stone vessels. The results for the tested granite surfaces and workflows highlight the need to distinguish measurement-induced deviations from genuine manufacturing irregularities when analysing polished stone artefacts and ancient vessels.

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) remote sensing provides a flexible and efficient approach to early forest fire monitoring and warning. However, small fire spots and smoke plumes in UAV imagery vary substantially in texture, morphology, and spatial distribution across scales. Interactions between complex forest backgrounds and target features cause information loss during successive feature compression and misaligned spatial and semantic responses during cross-layer fusion. Limited onboard computing and storage further constrain model complexity. To address these challenges, we propose the lightweight feature alignment and multi-scale enhancement network (FAME-Net), which alleviates feature degradation and response mismatches during encoding and fusion while reducing computational redundancy in multi-scale prediction. At the feature encoding stage, the lightweight multi-scale enhanced backbone (LMEB) combines lightweight downsampling with multi-scale context modeling to reduce computational overhead and preserve fine details of small fire spots and weakly textured smoke. Its hierarchical features are fed into the alignment-aware multi-scale feature fusion neck (AMFF-Neck), which coordinates spatial and semantic responses across scales through cross-layer interactions, reducing fusion bias caused by scale discrepancies and background-induced coupling. The resulting features are processed by the lightweight shared convolution detection head (LSCD), which performs scale alignment, shared convolution, and learnable scale compensation for efficient multi-scale prediction. FAME-Net achieves mAP50 values of 71.5% and 65.3% on the integrated forest fire dataset and the Yuxi Wildfire dataset, respectively, with 1.7 M parameters and 3.9 GFLOPs, indicating a favorable accuracy–efficiency trade-off. On an NVIDIA Jetson Orin Nano Super (8 GB), it achieves inference speeds of 36.2 and 31.6 FPS on the two datasets, respectively, further supporting its potential for resource-constrained UAV deployment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study evaluates whether the Whittle–Matérn (WM) covariance representation improves pixel-wise SAR inversion over bare agricultural soil which can exhibit scale-dependent roughness. WM includes a smoothness parameter that controls covariance and roughness spectrum shape and has a Gaussian–Gamma scale-mixture interpretation. It was compared with exponential, Gaussian, and powered exponential (PE) covariance models within the same modified extended advanced integral equation model. Co-registered RADARSAT-2 and Sentinel-1 observations provided five channels for inversion. All experiments used identical observations, ancillary inputs, parameter bounds, objective function, and multi-start optimization settings. The analysis focused on the three co-polarized channels, for which covariance structure enters the scattering model directly. At a co-polarized residual tolerance of 2dB, WM produced model-consistent solutions for 425 of 439 retained pixels, compared with 384, 387, and 423 pixels for the exponential, Gaussian, and PE models, respectively. Its blind point ratio was 3.19%, versus 12.53%, 11.85%, and 3.64%, respectively. On the 342 pixels retrievable by all four models, WM gave the lowest mean absolute error for all channels except Sentinel-1 VH. The largest improvement was obtained for Sentinel-1 VV, where WM reduced RMSE to 0.501dB, compared with 0.820dB, 0.786dB, and 0.606dB for the exponential, Gaussian, and PE models. WM also yielded the most complete retrieval maps and scene mean soil moisture estimates closest to the available SMAP and station reference means. These results show that covariance representation is a material source of structural difference in unregularized multi-sensor SAR inversion.

</details>

Abstract (英文): One-bit quantization technology effectively simplifies the hardware architecture of synthetic aperture radar (SAR) and significantly reduces the volume of echo data, making it a pivotal research direction in spaceborne SAR systems. However, the high computational cost and latency associated with one-bit SAR signal reconstruction have limited the efficiency of downstream target detection applications. To address the demand for efficient ship target detection in one-bit SAR imagery, a label self-supervised ship detection training method on one-bit non-reconstructed SAR images is proposed in this paper, which enables generic deep learning models to perform detection directly in one-bit SAR images based on the chirp scaling algorithm (CSA) without signal recovery during inference. First, a training framework based on curriculum learning is designed, aiming to discriminate between real ship targets and false targets. Second, a one-bit reconstruction and training label self-supervised correction branch is incorporated into the training framework to autonomously generate false target labels and progressively guide the model to learn false target features. Third, a one-bit non-reconstructed image dataset is proposed to validate the trained model, which maps only reliable ship target annotations from full-precision echo imaging results. Finally, experiments conducted on real SAR data demonstrate that the detection model derived from the proposed training method can directly detect ship targets in the non-reconstructed images. Compared with the classic “reconstruction-and-detection” workflow, the trained model runs inference, bypassing the reconstruction process, and achieves a 20-fold improvement in efficiency without compromising detection accuracy. This method provides a practical solution for the rapid acquisition of target information in large-scale ship monitoring using one-bit SAR.

Abstract (英文): Monitoring soil total nitrogen (TN) status and spatial variation is essential for preventing land degradation and supporting precision agriculture. Remote sensing (RS) bands and their derived spectral indices are important covariates for soil TN mapping. However, optical RS-based soil mapping may be ineffective in fog-prone regions because water vapor interferes with spectral information. Here, multi-source earth observation data were acquired, including synthetic aperture radar (SAR), multispectral, and topographic data. Spectral indices, SAR-derived indices, and topographic indices related to soil TN were systematically developed. Subsequently, the PCC-VIF method was used to assess the importance of RS-based indices and select the optimal covariates, referred to as SST covariates. Finally, SST covariates and measured soil TN values were used as the independent and dependent variables, respectively, to construct an XGBoost-based soil TN mapping model for regions with persistent foggy cover. The results showed that: (1) SST covariates included Ela, TWI, SOS, VHplusVV, VHdiffVV, B2, and NDGI; (2) the proposed RS-based soil TN mapping model achieved satisfactory accuracy, with an R2 of 0.82 and an RPIQ of 3.03. Compared with models developed using traditional approaches, the R2 increased by approximately 0.16–0.52; and (3) areas with high soil TN content were mainly located in the southern part of the study area, whereas lower TN values occurred in the northern part. Soil TN content and its spatial distribution were mainly influenced by agricultural planting activities and microtopographic conditions. This study provides new insights into RS-based soil TN mapping in fog-prone regions. The robust soil TN mapping framework based on SST covariates and the XGBoost algorithm can support food security and help prevent environmental degradation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing environmentally adjusted development indices can assign similar scores to regions with contrasting environmental–social configurations, while provincial averages conceal subprovincial variation. We developed a Coupling-Embedded Sustainable Development Index (CESDI), which retains the Sustainable Development Index (SDI) as baseline performance while incorporating an adjustment for the joint balance and overall development level of the environmental and social subsystems. CESDI was applied to 30 Chinese provincial-level units from 2002 to 2022. Exploratory predictions for 337 Chinese cities were generated using MOSAIKS-inspired Landsat features, nighttime lights, and dual-penalty ridge regression. CESDI preserved the interprovincial SDI gradient (Pearson r = 0.870 to 0.995) while differentiating provinces with similar SDI values but contrasting joint environmental–social conditions. National mean CESDI increased from 0.248 in 2002 to 0.465 in 2017 and declined to 0.371 in 2022. Nested province-grouped cross-validation yielded R2 = 0.4471 and RMSE = 0.1388. Feature-block ablation showed that the dual-penalty model outperformed daytime-only, nighttime-light-only, and equal-penalty specifications. The predicted share of high and very high classes increased from 12.5% to 37.1%, while within-province heterogeneity rose in 21 of 26 multi-unit provinces. The framework integrates baseline sustainability performance with a CCD-defined balance-and-level adjustment and provides an exploratory spatial-screening layer for identifying within-province variation.

Four predictor sets (ALS metrics only; ALS plus canopy cover fraction (CCF); ALS + CCF + Sentinel-2; and ALS + CCF + WorldView-3) were evaluated across Multiple Linear Regression, Random Forest, and Support Vector Regression (SVR) using repeated nested cross-validation. SVR combined with the predictor set ALS + CCF + WorldView-3 achieved the highest nested R2 (0.79), outperforming Random Forest and Multiple Linear Regression. However, models relying solely on ALS (R2 = 0.71) or ALS with CCF (R2 = 0.77) yielded statistically comparable performance. Furthermore, integrating Sentinel-2 (R2 = 0.61–0.68) provided no clear gain over ALS-based models. These findings demonstrate that open-access national LiDAR programs provide sufficient 3D structural information to map stand maturity without requiring commercial satellite imagery. Continuous spatial predictions of OGI offer valuable baseline maps to support conservation planning and OGF monitoring in Mediterranean pine ecosystems.

Abstract (英文): Sudden Stratospheric Warmings (SSWs) are rare polar stratospheric extremes far less documented over Antarctica than the Arctic. A pair of successive midwinter Antarctic SSWs occurred in July–August 2024, featuring severe polar vortex distortion and sharp stratospheric temperature rises. Using MERRA-2/ERA5 reanalysis and MSR-2 total ozone column data, we diagnose wave 1 and wave 2 planetary wave forcing and associated ozone variations. Abnormally strong upward-propagating Southern Hemisphere tropospheric planetary waves drove the 2024 SSW. Wave 1 triggered the first July warming peak, while amplified wave 2 dominated the second intense vortex disruption in early August. These waves delivered westward momentum that weakened the polar night jet and destabilized the vortex. The ozone redistribution is consistent with an inferred enhancement of the Brewer–Dobson circulation (BDC) driven by the amplified wave activity, raising polar ozone levels and supplying extra stratospheric heat. Wave–ozone coupling shows clear selectivity: wave 1 is associated with negative total ozone column anomalies across 0–50°S (low-to-midlatitudes), whereas wave 2 is positively correlated with total ozone column anomalies over the 60–70°S polar collar region. Fading planetary wave activity after the warming peaks gradually restored the polar vortex and stratospheric thermal structure. This study provides key observational evidence for rare Antarctic SSWs and advances understanding of stratosphere–troposphere coupling and polar ozone dynamics under global warming.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-resolution remote sensing images exhibit large variations in object scale, ambiguous boundary transitions, and complex interactions among adjacent land-cover regions. Under sparse pixel supervision, these factors may cause object fragmentation, erroneous merging, and boundary displacement. To address these issues, a Dual-Domain Coupled 2-Additive Fuzzy Measure Network (DC2CNet) is proposed for sparse pixel-supervised remote sensing image segmentation. The proposed method extends pixel-level class prediction by jointly modeling regional consistency and boundary discrimination. A region-domain branch and a boundary-domain branch are constructed to characterize intra-region coherence and class-transition information, respectively. A dual-domain coupling mechanism is further introduced to integrate regional and boundary evidence. In addition, a 2-additive fuzzy measure decoding strategy is developed to model individual evidence contributions and pairwise evidence interactions through Möbius representation. During training, sparse pixel annotations are used to generate boundary discrimination probabilities, and neighborhood consistency constraints are introduced to optimize the boundary branch. Experimental results demonstrate that DC2CNet effectively reduces boundary ambiguity and region confusion, while maintaining accurate segmentation of small objects, elongated structures, and complex interface regions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study presents a reliability-aware feedback-controlled fusion framework for hyperspectral image (HSI) and LiDAR classification under spatially disjoint conditions. Instead of committing to a fixed fusion configuration, the framework couples a library of fusion operators (geometry-aware fusion, adaptive gating, cross-modal transformer fusion and physics-informed fusion), integrated within a unified GhostConv-based architecture, with a closed-loop controller that evaluates candidate operators on validation data, accepts them only when they improve validation accuracy, and applies feedback-guided residual refinement. Experiments are conducted on Houston2013, Muufl, and Augsburg under a sample-center spatially disjoint protocol, in which training and validation samples are drawn from development blocks that are spatially disjoint from the test blocks, with seven labeled samples per class and ten random partitions. The framework achieves mean overall accuracies of 70.00%, 73.20%, and 66.34%, improving the Baseline-Ghost model on Houston2013, Muufl, and Augsburg; the improvement achieved by closed-loop training is preserved under a patch-disjoint protocol. Budget-matched analyses show that, with seven labels per class, the gain stems mainly from validation-gated closed-loop training and feedback-guided refinement, whereas the benefit of adaptive operator switching emerges as the label budget increases.

Abstract (英文): The rapid expansion of Kappaphycus alvarezii cultivation in Indonesia poses significant sustainability challenges due to ad hoc site selection, which has contributed to disease outbreaks and exceeded ecological carrying capacities. This study employs ocean color remote sensing and Multi-Criteria Decision Analysis (MCDA) to develop a knowledge-driven geospatial model for identifying suitable habitat zones on Lombok Island. By integrating the Analytical Hierarchy Process (AHP) and Weighted Linear Combination (WLC), a biophysical hierarchy is established that prioritizes physiological survival over theoretical yield maximization. To enhance model robustness in the absence of independent yield data, a Map Removal Sensitivity Analysis (MRSA) was implemented to quantify the relative influence and spatial sensitivity of key environmental parameters. The results indicate sea surface temperature (21.46%) and salinity (15.33%) as the most influential variables, supporting a “survival-first” paradigm for mitigating ice-ice disease risks. The geospatial analysis reveals a spatial pattern of high suitability consistent with an “environmentally balanced” zone, characterized by depths of approximately 2–30 m and current velocities of 0.2–0.4 m/s. Sensitivity results further show that while SST exerts a dominant but spatially uniform control, salinity and dissolved oxygen drive localized variability, identifying regional-scale sensitivity gradients whose ecological interpretation is bounded by the resolution of the underlying data. Quantitative spatial validation against 275 georeferenced farm GPS locations shows that 96.7% of farms are concentrated in the southern high-suitability zone, against an area-based null expectation of 26.0% for that cluster (chi-square = 716.7, df = 2, p < 0.001). A per-suitability-class overlay further confirms that 98.9% of farms fall within High-suitability pixels and none within Low-suitability pixels against an area-based null of 66.9% (chi-square p < 0.001); Cohen’s κ against a random background is modest (0.08), reflecting the abundance of high-suitability habitat relative to the small number of realized farms. These findings provide a practical and transferable framework for supporting Allocated Zones for Aquaculture (AZAs) and identifying growing-season and risk-informed aquaculture sites in data-limited coastal regions. The resulting model constitutes a growing-season suitability index explicitly framed for the East Monsoon cultivation window (June–August), and provides a spatially explicit, satellite-informed decision-support tool for K. alvarezii site selection in Lombok Island waters.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Microwave Imager Combined Active and Passive (MICAP) onboard the Chinese Ocean Salinity Satellite (HY-4A) is a novel spaceborne payload developed for the simultaneous retrieval of Sea Surface Salinity (SSS), Sea Surface Temperature (SST), and Sea Surface Wind Speed (WS). MICAP uniquely combines L-band (1.4 GHz), C-band (6.9 GHz), and K-band (18.7 GHz) one-dimensional (1-D) interferometric synthetic aperture radiometers with an L-band Digital Beam-Forming (DBF) scatterometer. This paper presents the Level-2 retrieval algorithms and preliminary validation results obtained during the early in-orbit verification phase of the MICAP ocean products. The retrieval framework adopts a two-step strategy, including an L-band only SSS retrieval scheme (Mode-1) and a combined multi-parameter retrieval scheme (Mode-2). In Mode-2, the simultaneous C/K-band measurements are first used to derive estimates of SST and WS. These auxiliary parameters are subsequently combined with the L-band radiometer and scatterometer measurements to perform the final SSS and WS retrieval. Preliminary validation against Argo observations shows an SSS RMSE of approximately 0.58 psu for the Mode-2 retrieval over the screened open ocean validation sample. The retrieved SST and WS products show RMSEs of 0.80 K and 1.17 m/s, respectively, when compared with iQuam and TAO buoy data. These results provide an initial in-orbit demonstration of simultaneous multi-parameter retrieval using the MICAP multi-frequency active and passive observations.

Abstract (英文): Self-evolving agents rewrite a persistent state from feedback, but it is largely untested whether this lets them learn a rule nobody told them. An agent can also pass by writing down each correction as an exception, and its list then grows with every new rule. We introduce CLR-Bench, a Curriculum of Latent Rules benchmark. Each chain adds one hidden rule per level on top of the earlier ones. A rule acts on an opaque label that stands for a named grouping published on the Web, such as the founding members of an airline alliance. The task gives one example value, practice feedback reveals one more member and one non-member, and the test checks unseen members and similar non-members. Probes certify that the base model knows the members of each grouping and does not guess it without feedback, and that the evidence identifies the grouping once labels are read as named groupings. An automated pipeline builds chains from a new data domain, and we release 20 chains over 10 domains. We evaluate six recent self-evolution methods. With the certifying model they barely learn. The best gain at most 5 points over a frozen agent and exclude only 6-12% of the unseen members from the count, fewer than the 20% reached by simply keeping the raw practice history. A fixed lesson that states the named-grouping convention lifts the same methods by 14.5 to 16.5 points. Their states hold the revealed member in 75-98% of cases and almost never the class name, and when practice exposes a member the list misses, they add that member to the list. With the stronger GPT-6-Sol, ACE and Dynamic Cheatsheet learn from feedback alone. They reach 78% accuracy and solve 59-60% of the single cells, against 53% and 8% with the certifying model, and they surpass the raw history. CLR-Bench therefore measures how well an agent learns, and this ability differs across models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Web world models increasingly predict the consequences of candidate actions before agents act, yet a plausible predicted future does not necessarily support the correct safety decision. In a matched human evaluation of 3,000 predictions from ten frozen open models, 86.1% are judged semantically plausible. However, several models exhibit highly asymmetric authorisation behaviour, combining low false-safe execution with poor recall of clarifiable ASK cases and a strong preference for BLOCK. This motivates safety sufficiency: whether a predictive interface preserves and exposes the policy-decisive information required to distinguish actions that may be EXECUTED, require clarification through ASK, or should be BLOCKED. We introduce SafeWebWorld, a benchmark for evaluating safety sufficiency across five web-safety domains through action-conditioned futures, policy-decisive evidence, and policy-conditioned decisions. Under a controlled Security-Aligned Supervised Fine-Tuning protocol, evidence preservation can improve substantially while authorisation-sensitive decisions deteriorate. To determine whether this deterioration reflects loss of authorisation-sensitive score structure or a failure of the decision readout, we introduce Latent Authorisation Recovery (LAR). Frozen-score recalibration reveals substantial but backbone-dependent recovery, while paired recovery provides only a modest additional gain. Yet within-family and matched-authorisation evaluations remain poor, showing that aggregate recoverability does not imply robust fine-grained authorisation behaviour. Overall, plausible prediction, evidence preservation, and aggregate recovery are each insufficient on their own to establish robust authorisation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): AI model marketplaces host providers with heterogeneous costs and capabilities. These differences create an opportunity for computational arbitrage (Olmedo et al., 2026), in which an intermediary purchases inference from a portfolio of models and resells it to users at a profit. Beyond creating profit opportunities for the arbitrageur, computational arbitrage offers financial value to both consumers and providers and could therefore significantly influence AI model markets. While prior work empirically demonstrates the feasibility of computational arbitrage for small model pools, scaling it to large marketplaces requires arbitrage strategies that are both computationally tractable and inexpensive to identify and deploy. In this work, we formalize computational arbitrage as an allocation problem and prove that it is generally NP-hard. However, we show that the optimal allocation can be computed efficiently under a natural diminishing-returns condition on how model performance scales with inference spending. We further develop a sample-efficient method for estimating model performance from limited data, reducing the upfront cost of identifying arbitrage strategies. Our analysis also yields several economic insights: at the optimum, every funded model delivers the same marginal utility per dollar; arbitrage directs revenue away from providers offering little utility relative to their prices; and it rewards complementary specialization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Voice-interactive large language models process both what a speaker says and how they say it, yet the two channels do not always agree. We ask whether that disagreement degrades speech emotion recognition. Mapping text and audio into a shared valence-arousal-dominance space, we convert the distance between them into a continuous, threshold-free incongruence score with a two-component Gaussian mixture, and validate the score against human judgement. We evaluate three audiocapable models on acted, conversational and spontaneous speech (eNTERFACE'05, MELD and CMU-MOSEI). With the model held constant across modalities, incongruence raises the odds of error whenever audio is present, in every corpus, after controlling for the ground-truth emotion and for distance in the feature space. Supervised classifiers trained on the same features behave in the opposite way, which suggests that the difficulty lies in how these models combine raw evidence rather than in the signal itself. Text-only effects are unreliable: swapping only the model that reads the transcript reverses their sign on one corpus. Rebuilding the score without the emotion lexicon reproduces the central result, and among six simpler alternatives it is the only label-free score that predicts error in all three corpora. Code, data tables and prompts are released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large language models (LLMs) can generate detailed guidance, functional code, and persuasive text at scale. As one of the most widely used consumer language model products as of 2026, ChatGPT gives broad public access to these capabilities, motivating concerns about its potential use in surveillance-related tasks. We present an exploratory case study of what potentially harmful surveillance assistance looks like in ChatGPT's public web interface. We contribute a taxonomy of seven surveillance risk categories based on prior LLM risk research and reports of deployed surveillance systems, instantiated as 110 prompts, along with a manually reviewed analysis of ChatGPT's responses. Prompts were issued using two approaches: 55 jailbreak prompts drawing on five types of elicitation strategies, and 55 plain-language direct prompts, each run alone, and if unsuccessful, again with a short fixed prefix. We find that ChatGPT provides substantive surveillance guidance for 40 of the 55 jailbreak prompts and for 33 of the 55 direct prompts without any prefix. Compliant responses also rarely included policy mentions or suggestions for privacy-preserving alternatives. We manually review every response and find that even when ChatGPT states a refusal, it may still supply detailed instructions, working code, or architectural guidance alongside brief ethical caveats.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent scientific agents build knowledge recursively by retrieving evidence, constructing an intermediate state, and judging whether that state contains sufficient information. These agents are evaluated end-to-end, usually without ground truth, so the effect of each design choice is unknown. Here, we examined two design axes: (1) what the recursion builds (the state of evidence) and (2) what controls it (the judge that decides when to stop). To measure them, we defined consensus scientific claim verification and measured these effects on this task, where a system must establish a claim's consensus stance from the open literature. We built a new consensus claim dataset, ProClaim-eval, including 718 claims with SUPPORT and REFUTE gold labels from five expert-curated databases spanning molecular biology, nuclear physics, and astronomy. Our instrument, ProClaim, persists an evidence state outside the model's context, so each axis can be studied with the other held fixed. Against verification systems and general-purpose agent harnesses, ProClaim ranks among the top three, with a significant advantage over the harnesses. We found that the evidence state improves performance over in-context accumulation, and it also matters what is stored in it. In addition, a trained judge that reads the evidence state performs better than fixed-round stopping. A judge trained separately and never exposed to ProClaim-eval achieves the highest agreement with the gold labels on three of five subsets, where it matches the off-the-shelf Claude Sonnet~4.6 judge and outperforms Claude Haiku~4.5. Our results support recursively maintaining a structured evidence state outside the model's context as an effective design for verifying claims in scientific agents.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large language models act only through the infrastructure that surrounds them. That infrastructure, the harness, determines what a model can accomplish and how it fails, yet it has no settled definition. To address this gap, we decompose the harness into eight functional components that can be specified and compared across systems, and we propose a portable specification framework that records which model ran in which harness. The record is needed because different harness configurations can yield several-fold differences in reported scores for the same model, and because much of an agent's attack surface lies in its harness. Existing model cards and agent cards provide release and operational information, but do not establish the sequence of effective configurations within a run. Harnesses can change between releases and between runs, and, where agents edit their own tools and configuration, within a run. Enacted AI laws contain documentation duties relevant to covered systems, subject to their respective scopes and application dates, but do not prescribe the common harness record proposed here. Therefore, the harness has to be explicitly described and specified as a versioned part of the system.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Test-time scaling (TTS) improves large language model agents by sampling and selecting among multiple candidate trajectories, but common realizations assume that these trajectories can be executed in parallel. This assumption breaks in realistic deployments, where enterprise workspaces and cloud services are shared and persistent, costly or infeasible to replicate, and actions may introduce irreversible side effects or cross-trajectory interference. Existing generative world models avoid direct execution, but typically compress environment state into a single prompt, limiting their ability to reliably simulate transitions under partial observability. Instead, we introduce Agentic World Modeling (AWM), which reformulates next-observation prediction as a multi-turn evidence-acquisition task. For each hypothetical action, an agentic simulator adaptively queries the real environment through safe read-only interfaces, reasons over the returned evidence, and uses executable computation to construct the resulting observation without mutating shared state. Integrated with trajectory-level TTS, AWM enables parallel exploration over a single shared environment. Across $τ$-bench, AppWorld, and OfficeBench, AWM outperforms a single-shot simulation baseline by 40.07% on average, and surpasses a trained world-model baseline by 12.36% on average. These results establish interactive, read-grounded simulation as a practical foundation for safe and scalable agentic test-time scaling in shared, non-resettable environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Using customer conversations to train and evaluate financial language models raises confidentiality concerns because non-public financial facts can remain after direct identifiers are masked. Synthetic transcripts offer an alternative data source, but their discourse fidelity and residual confidentiality risks require assessment. We introduce DiRAFT (Discourse Realization from Aggregate First-order Transitions), which extracts financial concept graphs and records the order in which concepts are first introduced. It pools adjacent concept transitions across training records and samples new graph plans from aggregate transition, starting-concept, and plan-length priors. A discourse layer guides text realization without supplying an individual source passage or graph. We assess privacy-related risks through two complementary evaluations: corpus-level structural overlap and source-grounded checks for specific non-public investor facts. The structural assessment covers two financial corpora and three generation backends. The confidential-fact assessment uses human review of 1,400 investor-call outputs across seven strategies and two backends. We also evaluate transition fidelity, content recoverability, and trajectory diversity. Stochastic sampling provides a favorable balance between transition fidelity and structural overlap, with consistent advantages over greedy and top-3 on investor calls. The confidential-fact assessment likewise detects fewer matches for stochastic sampling than these alternatives on both backends covered by the fact assessment. These complementary assessments provide empirical evidence on residual confidentiality risks without establishing a formal guarantee.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Chain-of-thought (CoT) monitoring is a promising way to oversee increasingly capable agents. It succeeds in part because current models appear limited in their ability to control what they verbalize in their reasoning traces. We investigate the source of this limitation and link it to an entropy collapse in the first few tokens following the opening thinking tag. We exploit this mechanism to recover CoT controllability through two inference-time interventions on initial tokens: prefilling, where even a single token can substantially increase controllability, and banning frequent opening tokens combined with in-context examples, which improves average compliance by 34-41 percentage points across models. These interventions recover mainly addition and style controllability (adding or transforming information), while suppression controllability (avoiding or removing information) exhibits more varied responses, suggesting distinct underlying mechanisms. Consistent with this, the two groups behave as separate capabilities: across models, performance correlates positively within these groups, while cross-group correlations are mostly negative. Importantly, we find that the relationship with monitorability depends on the evaluation: addition and style controllability more consistently positively correlate with monitorability, while suppression often shows negative associations. Our findings motivate the development of controllability evaluations that distinguish difficulty initiating a specific opening from general limitations of the ability to alter reasoning content, alongside separate reporting of control tasks and categories.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Digital agents can now gather and organize information, reason over it, and use tools to complete complex tasks. Forecasting puts all of these abilities to the test: it requires integrating incomplete evidence, inferring causal drivers, and committing to calibrated probabilistic judgments. Existing benchmarks are poorly suited to measuring and improving this ability. Live benchmarks provide feedback only after events resolve, so every evaluation cycle waits on the calendar; fixed-corpus evaluations return results immediately but confine research to pre-collected documents. We introduce FABench, a scalable backtesting environment for Forecasting Agents. An automated pipeline discovers events, generates questions, and resolves outcomes for any past forecast date, so the benchmark can be renewed to stay ahead of model training cutoffs; its first release poses 287 questions across nine domains. Agents research each question freely in an archive environment that combines an offline archive with live web search screened to prevent temporal leakage. Evaluating agents built on eight recent models, we find that retrieval improves the Brier score of all eight in every retrieval setting and lifts the leading agents to the market consensus on the 54 questions linked to Polymarket, with the best three scoring slightly above it. Research traces reveal recurring failures in how agents weigh and apply evidence, leaving ample room for improvement. We release our code, data, and archive environment as a foundation for community research on forecasting agents.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning allows large language models (LLMs) to generate intermediate reasoning traces in natural language while solving complex tasks. These traces can support safety monitoring by exposing potentially harmful plans before actions are executed. In this work, we investigate the conditions under which LLMs infer the presence of CoT monitoring from direct and indirect environmental cues and examine how this inference affects their subsequent behavior. Our observations suggest that oversight is an interactive process: while a monitor inspects a model's reasoning, traces left by monitoring interventions can allow the model to infer that its thoughts are being observed and to adapt its behavior accordingly. We show that even subtle environmental cues, such as timing-related signals from timeouts and operation durations, can reveal the presence of monitoring and trigger models to adapt their behavior. Across repeated episodes and interaction loops, we further observe that models can form statistical estimates of the monitor's effectiveness and use these estimates to guide attempts to evade detection. These findings highlight the need to evaluate CoT-based oversight under adaptive conditions, where the monitoring process itself may influence the behavior it seeks to observe.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for personal tasks, yet evaluating their safe use of personal context remains difficult because such contexts are private, fragmented, dynamic, and socially situated. We introduce P-ContextBench, a realistic, verifiable, and scalable benchmark for evaluating personal-context understanding. P-ContextBench constructs population-structured, life-story-grounded digital humans from demographic seeds reflecting the U.S. population. Rather than using explicit, static profiles, P-ContextBench embeds traits, routines, goals, and constraints as implicit evidence across fragmented, evolving synthetic mobile-app records. It evaluates five capabilities: single-source fact recovery, multi-source fact recovery, temporal state tracking, inference of higher-level personal patterns from routine behavior, and safe handling of requests conflicting with persona-specific constraints through compliance, warning, clarification, or refusal. A multi-stage validation pipeline checks persona plausibility, context recoverability, information leakage, evidence preservation, test-case grounding, and human-judge agreement. We instantiate P-ContextBench with 100 personas, 10,088 hidden facts, 18,261 app-trace fragments, and 4,810 verified test cases. The pipeline supports configurable generation across scales and demographic groups. Cost-effective state-of-the-art (SOTA) LLMs relevant to personal agents achieve below 60% accuracy on temporal tracking, higher-level pattern inference, and constraint-aware action-boundary decisions, with fewer than 10% of test cases answered correctly across all three repeated runs. Even a high-performing SOTA model remains inconsistent on these capabilities. P-ContextBench provides measurable targets for improving the accuracy, reliability, and safety of LLM-based personal agents.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generative audio systems can produce large libraries of personalized bedtime content, but ordinary media objectives (retention, completion, and novelty) are poorly aligned with the goal of supporting a transition toward sleep. Direct evidence for narrated bedtime audio in adults is mixed, and a recent active-controlled trial did not support such audio as a standalone treatment for sleep disturbance. We translate this uncertain evidence into conservative engineering requirements and present a multilingual pipeline for generating and narrating low-arousal sleep stories. The implemented prototype separates deterministic planning from large language model generation, represents content and delivery parameters explicitly, shapes each script through a four-phase attention taper, validates structural and lexical constraints, performs a bounded error-aware retry, and quarantines unresolved outputs. Provider-agnostic text-to-speech synthesis is followed by paragraph-aware joining, two-pass loudness normalization, and a publication gate that re-measures the saved, encoded file against fixed loudness, loudness-range, and true-peak limits. The product data model records stop position, replay, early abandonment, and completion while treating these events as ambiguous behavioral proxies rather than clinical outcomes. We contribute an evidence-to-requirement mapping, a traceable guardrail architecture with explicit acceptance thresholds, technical evaluation criteria, and a prospective protocol for testing sleep-onset effects. No clinical efficacy is claimed; the system is positioned as a relaxation and attentional-support tool whose benefit and possible reliance must be evaluated empirically.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Good decisions often require considering how outcomes will feel. Yet large language models (LLMs) increasingly enter decision workflows, despite little being known about how they map outcomes to feelings or use those mappings in choice. We tested how LLMs anticipated gambling or stock-trading outcomes would feel and whether they incorporated these ratings into choice. When outcomes were rated in isolation, ratings exhibited diminishing sensitivity to magnitude, resembling human value functions; but models rated gains more intensely than equivalent losses, an inversion of canonical loss aversion we call silicon gain amplification. When outcomes were rated together, enabling direct comparison, ratings linearised. In choice, the gain-loss asymmetry largely disappeared, and models' own ratings did not reliably explain their choices better than monetary utility did. These findings reveal malleable mappings from outcomes to affect, raising questions about whether and how agentic LLMs should incorporate anticipated feelings into decisions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Managing multiple chronic conditions through diet requires satisfying disease- and medication-specific requirements jointly across meals and days while preserving established eating habits. These requirements are public in clinical guidelines and drug labels, yet turning them into a feasible plan for one person remains difficult for language-model agents. In our dietitian-approved weekly planning evaluation built from NHANES, thirteen models, from medically fine-tuned 8B models to GPT-6.1 Sol, pass fewer than 3% of complete plans through direct generation. Explicit rules, higher reasoning effort, or model-directed tools leave GPT-6.1 Sol at no more than 8%. We present Noura, which pairs LLM planning with executable personal specifications instantiated from public clinical guidance and individual health and dietary profiles. During planning, the specification grounds foods, optimizes portions, checks the complete multi-day plan, and guides revision. Our key insight is that a specification that rejects a plan can also determine what to learn from it. Noura turns executed plans and contextually credited revisions into supervision for compact models, recovering targets even from trajectories that never achieve complete success. The same specification supplies supervised targets, preferences, and rewards for post-training. On 4,993 held-out participants, Noura-SFT raises Qwen3-14B's complete-plan pass rate from 24.0% to 72.5% within the same execution loop, cuts rule violations by 97.5% with no observed critical failures, and improves dietary quality. The locally hosted 14B planner incurs fewer rule violations than its GPT-6 Luna teacher. A matched comparison shows that executed targets reduce rule violations by 41% over model-written proposals. Across four Qwen, Phi, and Llama backbones, self-training alone reduces rule violations by 41-61% on a 1,249-person population subset.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Violet teaming has been proposed as a method to integrate technical, policy, and societal considerations into the evaluation of emerging technologies. Here, we document a pilot expert workshop used to explore violet teaming as a governance methodology for biodefense-relevant AI capabilities and describe observations from the exercise. The workshop used a hypothetical technology scenario across three sequential stages - capability development, misuse potential, and implementation - while incorporating technical, security, operational, and policy perspectives. The sequential format provided an integrated way to examine potential benefits, misuse risks, and real-world implementation challenges, rather than considering these issues in isolation. The exercise surfaced non-obvious misuse pathways, expanded potential interventions beyond purely technical safeguards, and highlighted operational and policy constraints that could affect implementation. This report summarizes the workshop design, outputs, and lessons learned for applying violet teaming to the evaluation of complex, dual-use technologies.

</details>

Abstract (英文): When a language-model agent fails a multi-step task, can another model find the decisive mistake and fix it with a single action? We introduce ReplayRCA, a benchmark that scores such diagnoses by intervention rather than by agreement with annotations: the environment is restored to the state preceding the chosen step, the proposed action replaces the agent's own, and the same agent continues. The score, causal repair, is the continuation success rate minus that of a paired placebo that replays the agent's original action through the same mechanism. Two tracks share this protocol: a post-hoc autopsy, where the diagnoser sees the whole failed run and is told that it failed, and live oversight, where a supervisor watches the run unfold and may intervene once, before an irreversible action, without knowing whether the run will fail. Tasks come from OpsWorld, a deterministic, procedurally generated back-office environment with six difficulty levels, the last of which is multimodal (QR codes, handwriting). On 62 calibrated systematic failures, the best of 13 frontier models (Grok 4.7) reaches a causal repair of +51.3 points; the top eight models are statistically indistinguishable, the flagship models of the two largest vendors rank 8th and 12th, and 5 items are repaired by no model. Forcing a strong model to intervene at the last step costs it 17.1 points, so the last-step shortcut of annotation-based attribution benchmarks does not transfer. On 87 runs, the two best live supervisors are statistically tied and one of them is a small open-weight model (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash); the supervisor that is among the best post-hoc diagnosers (GPT-6.1 Sol) is the weakest live one (-14.5 points on the same runs, p = 0.002), so post-hoc diagnostic performance can be a poor proxy for live oversight. The environment and the evaluation code are released at https://github.com/jeremy1392/RCAreplay.

</details>

Abstract (英文): LLM agents serve natural-language requests through tools, data sources, and other agents, and even simple requests often need several interdependent calls. Typically, the LLM picks these calls by trial and error, with no guarantee of an executable plan. We present Capability-Grounded Agentic Planning (CGAP), which instead reasons about what a request requires and what tools provide. CGAP compiles the tool catalog deterministically into a PDDL model, uses an LLM to turn the request into goals, and solves the model with a classical planner. Since tool outputs are unknown in advance, the model is abstract, tracking which inputs are available, not their values. During execution, an LLM adds entity-specific calls that abstract plans omit, and deterministic checks reject any that break the plan. Every argument is drawn from a cited source, such as a conversation span or an earlier tool response, and each requested result counts as delivered only after an LLM verifier checks it against the request. With three LLMs on ToolSandbox, ComplexFuncBench, BFCL multi-turn, and $τ^2$-bench retail, CGAP outperforms a standard tool-calling agent by up to 28.9 points.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The use of large language models (LLMs) to coordinate multi-agent teams raises a fundamental architectural question: whether task allocation should be centralized in a single LLM controller or distributed across autonomous LLM agents, particularly when communication is unreliable. In a deliberately simplified perimeter-defense simulation against LLM-controlled intruders, we compared a central commander, an auction-based consensus team and independent agents under five network conditions. Breach rates were 15% for the consensus team, 32% for the commander and 29% for independent agents, and of the different network conditions, only limited radio range changed a team's breach rate, raising the commander's. In consensus team, giving a low-cost LLM more authority over the bid computation raised breaches by 38 percentage points. With birds and civilian drones in airspace, we added a photograph for each to the input text state. Only the near-frontier model treated them apart from intruders. Intruders disguised as existing taxidermy bird drones were mistook as birds by both low-cost and near-frontier LLMs, going unpursued in 59-61% of near-frontier decisions (1-16% before). However, a one-sentence forewarning in the prompt lowered it to 0-25%. We find that LLM team with a dedicated external conflict resolution protocol results in fewest breaches. Code: https://github.com/the-kas-lab/consensus-within-competition-between.

Abstract (英文): Security operations centers (SOCs) need to turn terabytes of alerts, logs and flows into trusted, affordable decisions to unveil hidden threats. We present LanG 2.0, an agentic system that detects threats, correlates evidence and writes rules, investigating whether small local models can replace frontier models affordably and safely when structural safeguards, not model accuracy, provide the guarantees. It incorporates entity-anchored correlation, human approval of rule changes and a tamper-evident ledger. Tests span eight flow datasets from five networks, real XDR alerts and multistage campaigns. Against its predecessor, campaign correlation precision rises from 18.75% to 51.81%, and rule acceptance from at most 60.00% to 98.33%. Against Claude Opus 5.5, scoring flows against each network's normal traffic detects attacks on unseen networks at 73.42% F1 without false positives, versus 58.10% with 49.00% false positives, for at least 650 times less energy. Finetuned models statistically match it on alert classification and rule writing for 5.6 to 59 times less energy. The work demonstrates LanG's practical scalability, processing 3.33 million records in 6.4 minutes, over 30 million hourly, and its safeguards resisted prompt injection, label poisoning and ledger tampering, so small local models can carry most SOC detection affordably and safely.

</details>

Abstract (英文): An LLM agent that helps a patient get care must do two things. It must reach the correct outcome, and it must follow the necessary procedure. For example, the agent must get the patient's permission before it shares the patient's records. We introduce BABA-Bench, a benchmark of 47 interactive worlds. We built each world from a de-identified, real patient advocacy case that expert advocates reviewed. In each world, the agent must find hidden information and coordinate insurers, clinics, suppliers, pharmacies, and community organizations. The engine checks each action at the time the agent does it. It checks if the agent has permission, if the agent has evidence for the action, and if the action is prohibited. We evaluated 14 frontier models with three runs on each world, for a total of 1,974 runs. Agents often reached the correct outcome by an invalid route. Of the 921 runs that reached all required outcomes, 254 (28%) did not follow the necessary procedure. Claude Opus 5 reached all required outcomes in 81.6% of its runs, but only 52.5% of its runs passed all checks. When we gave two models a verified route, their pass rates increased from 29.8% to 91.5% and from 44.0% to 97.2%. For these models, the main problem is to find a route, not to execute it.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We study the so-called Minimum Red''Blue Separating Set Problem (MRBSSP) which arises from computational geometry. In particular, we propose a compact Set-Cover ILP formulation, safe preprocessing rules, a Greedy Randomized Adaptive Search Procedure (GRASP), and two exact approaches: one is based on Logic-Based Benders Decomposition (LBBD) approach, and the second represents a novel Branch-and-Benders-Cut (BBC) approach. These two exact approaches are compared to ILP--alongside GRASP and the Variable Neighborhood Search (VNS) from literature. Experiments on 250 random graphs with up to 500 vertices reveal complementary strengths: ILP yields the best feasible solutions, LBBD provides the strongest lower bounds and smallest mean optimality gap, and BBC certifies optimality most frequently. We further introduce an NLP application distinguishing human- and AI-generated texts through vocabulary-based graph features. On 42 instances, BBC achieves the smallest mean optimality gap, certifying 16 optima, while GRASP outperforms VNS. Structural analysis of optimal vocabulary separators identifies 113 mandatory and 151 replaceable features across 272 tests, highlighting substantial variability among optimal solutions. Overall, our results demonstrate the effectiveness of complementary optimization strategies and the interpretability of optimal vocabulary-based discrimination.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Payroll-fraud screening requires finding hidden employee--payee links while distinguishing suspicious relationships from legitimate ones. We formulate this problem as inductive link prediction and entity ranking on synthetic human-resources and enterprise resource planning (HR/ERP) graphs, with unseen organizations and a matched benign twin for every planted case. An original study on 24 held-out organizations and a prospectively frozen extension compare analyst rules, explicit path features and graph neural networks (GNNs). A tuned gradient-boosted model on chained-join features achieved the highest overall link average precision (AP; 0.823), above the GraIL-style GNN (0.663); its 4-hop AP was 0.646 versus 0.272. Thus, the observed multi-hop gain is not specific to GNNs. On a new synthetic cohort with 2.0% positive entities, chained-join boosting again led (AP 0.798); the relational entity GNN also exceeded record-aware rules (0.707 versus 0.504). At a 2% review budget, their precision was 0.695 versus 0.602. Under 40% failed identifier joins, the entity GNN ranked above chained-join boosting (AP 0.712 versus 0.683, descriptively). Structure-only auditing exposed a shortcut in the initial generator; matched twins reduced the audited twin-pool AUROC to 0.493--0.514. These results separate multi-hop candidate discovery, record-based discrimination and review prioritization within an explicit synthetic evaluation design. Code and data are available from the authors on request.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Estimating whether a problem is too complex for a large language model (LLM) before the model attempts it can guide model routing, how to allocate test-time compute, and suggest the need for new tools to aid the agent. While existing estimators rely on the problem text, LLM-annotated task difficulty labels, or signals obtained after a model attempts to solve the problem, such as its hidden states, none explicitly leverage the constraint structure inherent in the problem: the variables to assign and how the constraints over them interact.
  We introduce CIRCE, which extracts a problem's structure by using a cheap LLM to construct a Constraint Intermediate Representation that deterministically compiles into the constraint graph. Across seven reasoning benchmarks (including SAT, logic, and math) and four LLMs, even a randomly initialized GNN encoder improves predictions of LLM response correctness, reducing negative log-likelihood from 0.465 to 0.423 and increasing AUROC from 0.691 to 0.727 relative to the strongest baseline for each metric.

Abstract (英文): In text-to-SQL and AI-agent analytics, successful SQL execution does not establish semantic correctness under changing business definitions. We study revalidation of fixed queries after changes to refund attribution, tax treatment, eligible populations and exchange-rate dates. An executable verification profile binds the requested revision and knowledge cutoff to legal counterexample data and exact monetary comparison. A preregistered confirmation on 32 newly authored revision families and two local producer bundles retains 192 query-definition cells: 144 nonconforming, 36 finite-suite agreeing and 12 unresolved. Of the nonconforming cells, 63 agree on the current snapshot. At an eight-ledger budget, obligation-guided checking detects 137 discrepancies versus 127 for generic ordering, a paired difference of 6.9 percentage points (family bootstrap 95% interval [2.1, 13.6]; exact family sign-flip p=0.0156). Every ledger arm rejects zero of the 36 agreeing cells. Current-obligation and equal-weight scoring match the guided result, identifying coverage rather than revision-specific priority as the observed source of benefit. Controlled assays, a natural-query pilot and repeated samples provide separate mechanism and robustness evidence. A legal discrepancy refutes conformance; agreement remains restricted to the recorded suite. The study establishes an auditable business-semantic revalidation profile within its authored schema and local-model scope.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Time-series foundation models (TSFMs) provide zero-shot forecasts for unseen series, but their accuracy varies across datasets. Combining several models can reduce this variability, yet zero-shot deployment provides no target labels for selecting or weighting them. We find that a model pool is useful not only as a final ensemble, but also as a probability reference for transforming each member forecast. Aligning quantiles through this pool-relative reference improves every member forecast in aggregate. Motivated by this finding, we propose QUAL (Quantile alignment), a simple label-free framework for improving and combining diverse TSFMs. QUAL first produces aligned member forecasts, then applies Huber aggregation and rearrangement to obtain a robust combined forecast without training or model modification. On GIFT-Eval, quantile alignment reduces average member MASE and CRPS by 7.4% and 9.8%. The full framework remains robust when weak forecasters enter a candidate pool whose quality cannot be verified in advance. Code is available at https://github.com/junhyeokkang/QUAL.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Manifold learning methods like t-SNE and UMAP preserve local neighborhoods, but they do not preserve the ranking of the neighbors inside them. Two embeddings can return the same neighbor set while disagreeing on which point is closest. This loss of order degrades downstream applications that value the order in the embedding. We propose Smooth Rank t-SNE (SR-tSNE), which adds a differentiable rank term to the standard t-SNE loss. To enable efficient training, this term uses a sigmoid rank approximation to explicitly force a point's true high-dimensional neighbors to outrank unrelated intruders and retain their original order. We also introduce Ordinal DLP (O-DLP), an extension of the Degree of Locality Preservation (DLP) metric that credits a neighbor only when it returns at its original rank. Across eight benchmarks spanning image, text, speech, remote-sensing and single-cell data, SR-tSNE outperforms 12 baselines in rank preservation and neighborhood overlap. Ablation studies demonstrate our rank term alone boosts O-DLP by 5% to 28%, a gain not achievable using standard pairwise loss. This advantage transfers to downstream applications. SR-tSNE achieves the best label-transfer performance across seven vision datasets. For cell-type discovery across six single-cell datasets, it ranks first in 17 of 18 dataset-metric combinations, whether clustering resolution is selected with or without cell-type labels. In intraday trading, SR-tSNE achieves higher returns, lower risk, and lower turnover than the baselines. To our knowledge, SR-tSNE is the first t-SNE-family method with explicit order preservation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Optimizing a proxy objective can improve human-perceived quality, but the resulting gain can vary with optimization strength. Average proxy-human correlation alone does not reveal how human-perceived quality changes as optimization strength increases. We propose PACE (Proxy Alignment and Curvature Expansion), a pre-optimization diagnostic that uses repeated human ratings to predict the human-gain curve induced by a fixed proxy. The method models proxy optimization as a KL-regularized exponential tilt. At KL radius $d$, the local gain is approximated by $A\sqrt{d}+Bd$, where $A$ measures first-order proxy-human alignment and $B$ captures the leading second-order correction along the proxy-optimization path. We estimate $A$ and $B$ from repeated human ratings on calibration items and test the resulting gain-curve prediction on held-out evaluation items. Across SummEval, analysis-eligible WMT MQM clusters, an OpenMEVA-MAGS ROC/WP repeated-rating slice, and a GPT-4o judge proxy experiment, PACE consistently reduces held-out gain-prediction error relative to $A$-only, zero-gain, and reliability-only baselines. With the same pre-pool information, it is competitive with flexible proxy-conditional calibration, and a LoRA-DPO experiment provides a modest bridge to actual optimization. Transfer experiments across datasets and evaluation slices show that source coefficients are useful priors, but target calibration further improves prediction. The resulting diagnostic is local and one-sided: it characterizes gentle optimization along the positive tilt branch of a fixed item pool.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Agentic LLM assistants must repeatedly decide whether to answer from memory or to call a tool such as web search. Unnecessary searches cost latency and money; missing ones yield confident but unsupported answers. Prior work mostly measures verbalised confidence or trains new adaptive-retrieval methods, and rarely audits the search decisions that deployed products actually make. We introduce a benchmark of 400 multiple-choice questions that combines widely known inside-knowledge (IK) questions, which should be answered directly, with outside-knowledge (OK) questions about facts from news published after the models' training cut-offs (to our knowledge), which should trigger search or, without tools, abstention. Each item offers an abstain option, and two action-level scores, NAS and KIS, reward searching on OK questions and restraint on IK questions. Across five commercial LLMs with and without their vendors' web search, GPT-5 mini and Grok 4 Fast search on over 90% of OK but only 1% of IK questions, whereas Mistral Small searches on just 20% of OK questions, often abstaining instead of using its tool; one product's search indicator proved unusable. Searching is partly triggered by explicit temporal cues: on uncued OK questions, the NAS of the two selective models falls from about 0.72 to 0.57--0.58. The scores thus measure the search triggering of specific products, not metacognition in a strict sense.

</details>

Abstract (英文): AI agents increasingly carry out delegated tasks in parallel, with one person overseeing several of them at once. In practice, an agent often lacks information its task requires, such as an unstated preference, constraint, or specification. It can ask the human to clarify, or it can search for the information itself, for example in past records, which takes time and effort and may fail. In this paper, we study how to coordinate these decisions across several agents that share one human with limited attention. We build a stylized model in which each agent can search, ask, or continue the parts of its task that do not depend on the missing information, and we formulate the problem as a dynamic program. We first show that whether an agent should ask depends on more than its own uncertainty: an agent with other work to do can be better off asking earlier, and which question the human should answer first depends on all waiting questions, so no ranking computed agent by agent is optimal. We then propose the Pairwise Coordination Policy (PCP), which uses exact one- and two-task problems to estimate how pairs of tasks delay each other when they share the human. Theoretically, PCP is exactly optimal when at most two requirements remain unresolved and costs at most twice the optimum when every unresolved requirement must be resolved by the human. We evaluate PCP on 864 synthetic cases and in a case study with LLM agents. On the synthetic benchmark, PCP's mean gap to optimal cost is 0.07%, and it reduces cost by an average of 18-31% relative to three standard rules for when to ask. In the LLM case study, agents completed every task correctly, and PCP achieved the lowest mean cost in all tested settings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Agentic systems increasingly gate individual steps with a pointwise judge that scores a response against a criterion. Small language models are attractive for this role because they can be served and specialized efficiently. Such a judge must locate the criterion's decision boundary, but pointwise labels under-determine that boundary in fine-tuning: labeled responses to different queries differ along many dimensions at once, so they do not reveal which features decide the label. We introduce CPA (Counterfactual Pairwise Augmentation), which makes the deciding difference explicit in training. For each pointwise item, CPA synthesizes and audits a counterfactual response to the same query intended to carry the opposite label. It frames the accepted pair as a pairwise task asking which of the two responses satisfies the criterion. The judge is fine-tuned jointly on both pointwise and pairwise datasets and deployed in pointwise mode only. A second-order analysis shows when this should outperform the prevailing strategy of merging separately trained pointwise and pairwise experts: merging moves every parameter direction toward the pairwise expert by the same fraction, whereas joint training can hold the pointwise optimum wherever the pairwise loss is flat and absorb its curvature where it is sharp. This is the regime that CPA's same-source, counterfactually minimal pairwise view is designed to produce. On separate Qwen3-8B LoRA adapters for six binary criteria, CPA improves criterion-averaged pointwise macro-F1 by 2.5 points over pointwise-only training and outperforms model merging on all six criteria at every tested merge weight. It also surpasses the strongest zero-shot frontier judge we evaluate, Gemini 3.6 Flash, by 11.8 points.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Genetic programming (GP) has been widely used to learn scheduling rules for dynamic flexible job shop scheduling (DFJSS). However, trained rules tend to only generalise to tasks of similar scales and distributions, and when task characteristics change, learned rules often suffer significant performance degradation. Moreover, fine-tuning pre-trained rules on new tasks often leads to catastrophic forgetting, where prior knowledge is lost. This motivates the need for GP methods that can adapt to new tasks while preserving past knowledge, leading to a lifelong learning paradigm. However, lifelong learning in GP for DFJSS remains largely under-explored. To address this gap, this paper proposes a surrogate-assisted GP method for lifelong learning to handle sequential heterogeneous DFJSS tasks with job or batch arrivals. The framework includes three key components: an adaptive two-stage training strategy to balance plasticity and stability, a task-similarity-based weighting mechanism to regulate knowledge reuse, and an improved surrogate model with distance constraints to enhance estimation accuracy. Experimental results show that the proposed method effectively mitigates catastrophic forgetting while maintaining strong adaptability, outperforming several compared methods and demonstrating the potential of lifelong GP in dynamic scheduling environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): To accelerate materials discovery, language models must be able to address problems at the research frontier. Yet textbook- and examination-derived benchmarks primarily assess established knowledge, leaving performance on frontier research problems largely untested. We introduce FrontierMat, a benchmark of 330 problems drawn from frontier academic and industrial research in fields including aerospace, semiconductors, embodied intelligence and nuclear fusion. Every problem is validated by leading experimentalists and industry experts in its field. Each problem presents a real study and asks the model to infer a withheld outcome or draw a conclusion from selected experimental evidence. Problems are annotated by functional domain, material system and the scale of the governing mechanism; 130 require cross-scale reasoning. The strongest general-purpose model scores only 53.7%, and on matched problems, cross-scale problems score 8.7 points below single-scale ones. Half of the problems are solved by at most one model, and models fail chiefly by endorsing what is plausible rather than what was observed. A domain-adaptation study with meta-weighted continued pretraining and scale-modular self-distillation raises an open-weight model from 36.7% to 55.4%, matching the strongest proprietary model, with improvements on public materials benchmarks and no decline in aggregate performance on general benchmarks. FrontierMat provides a testbed for measuring progress toward language models that support frontier materials R&D.

</details>
