Abstract (英文): Weight pruning can reduce the storage and inference cost of large language models (LLMs), but preserving quality at high sparsity levels remains challenging. Existing methods face a quality-cost trade-off in this regime. One-shot pruning is cheap but degrades sharply as sparsity increases, even when followed by post-pruning recovery. A growing line of work instead optimizes the mask directly from the dense model under the end-to-end objective; among these methods, [Lee et al.,2026], the recent state of the art at extreme sparsity, reaches high quality but at the cost of model-sized optimizer state and auxiliary variables. To address this gap, we propose ETAPS, which prunes gradually and adapts sparsely: it tightens a global sparsity constraint in progressive stages while training a sparse delta with a small, fixed budget. Our key observation is that gradual weight removal keeps each pruning step small enough for sparse adaptation to compensate, allowing the adapted model to provide end-to-end gradients that guide the next pruning decision without model-sized optimizer state. Across six models from 125M to 13B parameters, ETAPS achieves lower perplexity than ELSA at every evaluated sparsity from 50% to 90%, with perplexity reductions of up to 42%. ETAPS is also far cheaper: at 90% sparsity, it prunes LLaMA2-7B on a single H200, where ELSA uses four, with $5.0\times$ smaller total persistent state and 49% fewer GPU-hours. Together, our findings point to a promising direction for achieving high quality in demanding regimes such as extreme sparsity at substantially lower cost.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World-action models increasingly apply test-time scaling, spending extra inference computation on candidate selection and adaptive allocation to improve control. Such gains are credible only when candidates carry exploitable signal, nominal compute savings become real speedup, and evaluation stays reliable. We audit all three on Cosmos Policy and RoboCasa with a premise-first, pre-registered methodology. On clean data, a learned value selector is 1.66 pp worse than random; a hindsight oracle gains only 1.40 pp, with an episode-level interval spanning zero; and 82% of states show identical candidate outcomes, leaving little selection headroom. Token pruning cuts per-forward FLOPs by up to 47% but yields at most a measured 1.15$\times$ end-to-end speedup on the deployed path (1.25$\times$ on a decode-excluded path), short of a 1.5$\times$ target, and our adaptive-allocation experiment is inconclusive under a power gate. We further identify and fix a silent rendering corruption whose rate rises sharply along compute-heavy planning paths while leaving the official direct-policy result unaffected, and release a detector, fix, and scripts. These findings show why test-time scaling should be audited as a chain of premises (decision signal, realized efficiency, evaluationintegrity) rather than by success rate alone. Conclusions are scoped to the tested configuration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large-scale behavioral pretraining offers a promising route to generalizable representations in online payment systems. However, high-cardinality identity fields such as card, device, and account IDs, though rich in behavioral signals, remain underexploited. Their continually growing vocabularies and limited semantic meaning make existing vocabulary-based and semantics-based encodings ill-suited. Their generalizable signal lies not in specific values, but in the identity behavior patterns they exhibit across a transaction sequence. Building on this insight, we propose ID2Pattern, a pretraining framework that brings these patterns into transaction foundation models. ID2Pattern first encodes identity fields with a Local Anonymous ID (LAID) tokenizer, which captures how each identity appears and recurs within the sequence, independent of its specific value. It then pretrains a multi-scale pattern codebook on unlabeled sequences, which abstracts LAID sequences into discrete behavior patterns for downstream prediction. Experiments on an industrial dataset with over 100M sequences and six public datasets show that ID2Pattern consistently outperforms strong baselines, improving industrial by 4.13% and public datasets by 8.83% on average. Notably, ID2Pattern remains equally effective even when all identity values are unseen during training, highlighting its potential to generalize to new users and markets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Linear attention scales linearly with sequence length, but its finite-state memory can limit long-context retrieval. In gated linear recurrences, memory retention depends on state decay, which is shaped jointly by gate parameters A_log and dt_bias and by input activations. In controlled Gated DeltaNet (GDN) experiments, both parameters remain highly correlated with their initial values after training, highlighting the importance of initialization. Therefore, we introduce Dual-Axis Memory Initialization (DAMI), which jointly configures these parameters before training. DAMI assigns geometric timescales across heads for varied retrieval distances and a low-high-low input-sensitivity profile across layers. A small activation sample calibrates the GDN-family implementation to match both behavioral targets without adding trainable parameters. Across GDN, KDA, GDN2, Mamba-2, and RWKV-7, DAMI improves average RULER-style retrieval accuracy by 5.29 to 16.71 percentage points over the corresponding random-initialization baselines. Performance on language modeling, commonsense reasoning, and 2K real-world retrieval remains broadly comparable to those baselines. Our code is available at https://speedai.com/research/dami.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-turn negotiation remains a challenging problem for large language model (LLM) agents. While LLMs can conduct natural-language dialogue, existing benchmarks predominantly assess only agreement rates, overlooking whether negotiated commitments actually create value once executed. To address this gap, we introduce TradeWar, a multi-country simulation benchmark that translates negotiation dialogues into concrete tariff and trade-share policies, enforces capacity and network constraints during settlement, and evaluates outcomes via an Economic Health Index (EHI). To solve this execution-grounded task, we propose TACTIC (Trade-aware Adaptive Consolidation and Theory-of-mind for International Commerce), an agent that iteratively refines its negotiation strategy across episodes-without updating base LLM weights-by leveraging prior experience, opponent behavior, and past settlement outcomes. Despite using zero-shot chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting, PromptLLM attains a 75% agreement rate but only 57.1 final EHI (*100), whereas TACTIC reaches 68.8. Across 20 sequential episodes and three seeds, TACTIC's final EHI improves from 58.2 to 73.2, compared with 65.2 for an ablation without cross-episode updates. Under technology-controls and energy-crisis shocks, TACTIC also exhibits smaller performance degradation than PromptLLM and MemoryLLM. These results indicate that CoT deliberation and interaction memory alone do not fully address delayed execution feedback, whereas settlement-grounded adaptation improves long-horizon negotiation in TradeWar.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hundreds of millions of people now ask large language model (LLM) chatbots for health advice, which raises a question that is simple to pose and hard to answer: does everyone get the same advice? We audit six widely deployed LLMs from four providers on smoking cessation, a domain with codified clini- cal guidance and substantial public-health stakes, by putting the same four cessation questions to twelve user personas that differ in life stage, self-described expertise, and smoking history, for a total of 2,880 responses. What the models say depends on who appears to be asking. Every model named FDA-approved prescription cessation medications less often to teen personas than to adult personas, by 32 to 67 percentage points, and in five of six models a persona presenting health- care expertise received fewer facts than a persona presenting none. We then evaluate one deployable intervention: a short system prompt specifying a factual floor, the set of core med- ical facts every user should receive when they are relevant, while leaving tone, vocabulary, and framing free to adapt. The prompt narrows persona differences in every model, but its effect on overall fact coverage varies in both magnitude and direction across models, so a prompt validated on one system cannot be assumed to transfer to another. Two cautions frame these results. Equal mention rates are not always the correct clinical target, because prescription cessation pharmacother- apy is not recommended for minors, so a defensible factual floor must be age-aware rather than uniform. And because our personas vary on several attributes at once, we report scenario- level differences rather than isolated effects of age or expertise. We release all 2,880 responses and the analysis code.

</details>

Abstract (英文): AI answers require more than apparent correctness: reviewers need to inspect their recorded basis. We introduce the Knowledge Layer Model (KLM), an open, implementation-neutral specification for inference records with versioned epistemic assessments, evidence relations, and explicit unknowns. SEDIM, the knowledge and evidence architecture of Nage AI OS, is a reference implementation. We reference KLM v0.3 (repository tag v0.3.0). Empirical material consists of separate internal experiments, not end-to-end validation. A historical platform-domain routing benchmark reports 137/150 classifications across 15 classes (91.3%; nominal 95% Wilson interval 85.7-94.9%); the numerator is recovered from a rounded summary, and historical example membership is unverified. A native-model weight-path configuration yielded 0/15 successful answers in a corrected two-source test. A capacity-growth comparison could not establish a reliable effect because of a defective legacy scorer and missing original outputs. Lexical and scoped diagnostics each yielded AUC 0.008 for silent-versus-contradicted examples, below the registered minimum of 0.65. Legacy packing lost gold-containing text in 118/221 selected cases; repaired packing retained it in 221/221. These measure text presence, not answer quality. Python and TypeScript verifiers reportedly agreed on six checks per synthetic fixture; a separate production disclosure was not accepted. Restoration, re-derivation, human-rubric support, boundary isolation, and bounded-response evaluations remain proposed. Findings are internal and author-reported; historical observations are distinguished from later reproductions. Evidence visibility does not establish correctness, causal reliance, security, or regulatory compliance. Independent replication and a complete public audit package remain future work.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World action models (WAMs) learn latent variables from observations and use these to both predict the evolution of the scene and to generate actions. The prediction has to be of the whole scene, whereas only a portion of the scene causes actions. WAMs fitted to demonstrations do not, in general, guarantee that they will generate actions solely from the latent causes of the demonstrated actions (the action-relevant latent variables), as the model could just as well learn to imitate in this way whilst entangling the action-relevant latent variables with the nuisance variables. We therefore separate out what causes action from what prediction depends on. We prove that under a sparsity constraint the latent variables used to generate actions are an invertible function of the action-relevant latent variables alone and the true structure is identified up to a permutation of the latent variables. This requires neither sufficient temporal dependence nor any assumptions on how the unobserved regimes evolve and holds even when the action-relevant latent variables are statistically dependent on the rest. We therefore propose CWAM (Causal WAM), which incorporates the constraint into a pretrained WAM by adding a sparse routing path to its action head, with the world modeling objective remaining unchanged. Experiments on both synthetic data and real-world robots validate the theory as well as showcase the improved efficiency in closed-loop success.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In real-world deployments, large language model (LLM) systems are increasingly evolving from single-model invocation toward heterogeneous deployments in which different models are dynamically invoked for inference. When the same context is reused across models, however, each newly selected target model typically has to process it again, repeatedly incurring substantial computational costs. Therefore, we propose ACCT (Asymmetric Cross-LLM Context Transfer), which targets KV cache transfer between models of different scales within the same model family. ACCT transforms the KV cache produced by a source model into sparse KV states that can be directly consumed by a target model, thereby enabling cross-model reuse of context computation and decoupling context computation from downstream inference. ACCT consists of three lightweight components: the Cartographer identifies query-relevant context tokens for each target layer and KV head; the Allocator determines how many context tokens each target layer and KV head retains under a shared total KV budget; and the Compiler transforms selected source KV into sparse target KV. The target model can therefore perform inference using the transferred KV without reprocessing the original context. We evaluate ACCT in two complementary settings comprising five general-capability benchmarks and six highly context-dependent tasks. Experiments show that ACCT matches or exceeds the target model's original inference performance even when reducing the KV cache size by 75%. At a context length of approximately 19K tokens, ACCT's KV conversion achieves a 6.5-21.2 times speedup over target context prefill. These results demonstrate the feasibility of transferring context computation across models under a limited KV budget.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Agentic workflows are increasingly shifting towards long-horizon tasks, such as open-ended scientific work, where models benefit from long context windows. However, at ultra-long context (above 1M tokens), full attention is prohibitively expensive. A promising approach is sparse attention, in which a dedicated indexer selects a subset of keys for the attention. Yet current methods of indexing either still have quadratic cost in context size or lose accuracy at ultra-long context scale. We address this with Latent Sparse Attention (LSA), an efficient attention method that maintains accuracy at ultra-long context. In LSA, keys are hierarchically clustered in a learned latent space, and queries score the hierarchy of clusters instead of scoring every key, making prefill, decode and training faster at long context. We propose two variants, Distilled-LSA (D-LSA), which can be applied to pre-trained models via distillation, and Native-LSA (N-LSA), which is trained natively on the next-token loss alone. On GLM-5.3-FLASH (QWEN-3.8-27B) at 10M tokens, D-LSA speeds up one attention layer by 22.7x (72.4x) in prefill and 4.6x (42.5x) in decode over its original sparse attention (full attention), while keeping similar accuracy across a variety of tasks. N-LSA matches the accuracy of full attention with a training speedup of 1.6x per layer at 128k tokens. LSA offers practical and efficient attention for models to benefit from ultra-long context.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Lake ice phenology is a sensitive indicator of climate change, yet its regional hydroclimatic associations remain poorly understood. Here, we reconstructed daily lake surface water temperature (LSWT) from MODIS for 583 lakes across China and derived freeze-up date (FUD), break-up date (BUD), and ice cover duration (ICD) for 376 seasonally ice-covered lakes. Robustness was evaluated with sensitivity tests, meteorological observations, transition-period comparisons, and passive-microwave ice records; Mann-Kendall tests, Theil-Sen slopes, VIF-constrained XGBoost-SHAP, and structural equation modeling assessed trends and regional associations. FUD was delayed in all three ice-covered regions, whereas BUD was delayed in the Tibetan Plateau Lake Region (TPR) but advanced in the Mongolian-Xinjiang Lake Region (MXR) and Northeast Lake Region (NLR), resulting in shorter ICD in all regions. Regional mean ICD declined by 0.03, 0.35, and 0.47 d yr -1 in the TPR, MXR, and NLR, respectively; significant lake-level changes were concentrated in the TPR, with 72 and 33 lakes showing significant FUD and BUD trends. All nine models retained positive hold-out R 2 (0.535–0.960). SHAP attributions differed by region: radiative variables dominated in the TPR, the MXR showed metric-specific associations with wind, snowfall, geographic, and thermal , and geographic, radiative, and morphometric variables dominated in the NLR. Structural equation models indicated stronger precipitation-related associations in the NLR and snowfall-related associations in the MXR and TPR. These results reveal pronounced regional heterogeneity in lake ice responses to warming and the value of integrating remote sensing, interpretable machine learning, and pathway modeling to resolve these contrasts.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Spaceborne Global Navigation Satellite System Reflectometry (GNSS-R) has shown great potential for terrestrial monitoring, but the compensation of small-scale surface roughness remains an important challenge. In particular, the root-mean-square surface height σ h , its incidence angle dependence represented by n in c o s n θ , and their relationships with soil moisture (SM) are still insufficiently characterized for spaceborne GNSS-R. In this study, CYGNSS surface reflectivity (SR) observations are combined with model-simulated smooth bare-soil reflectivity derived from SMOS SM and vegetation attenuation represented by SMOS-IC L-band Vegetation Optical Depth (L-VOD). Global σ h and n are estimated on a 25 km grid by fitting the model predictions to CYGNSS observations. The retrieved σ h is compared to multiple topographic variables, SMAP and SMOS-IC roughness parameters, and Saharan aerodynamic roughness measurements, while the dependence of n on vegetation and land cover is also investigated. Results show that the retrieved σ h is more strongly associated with terrain descriptors such as uniformity, Shannon index, entropy, Northness, and Eastness than with conventional elevation- and slope-based variables. Both σ h and n also exhibit relationships with SM and vegetation, indicating potential coupling between effective roughness characterization and vegetation-related uncertainties. After compensation for vegetation and roughness effects, CYGNSS SR shows good consistency with simulated bare soil SR. Additional evaluation using in situ SM observations from the International Soil Moisture Network further supports the effectiveness of the retrieved parameters. Overall, these findings offer valuable insights into the use of physics-based empirical or semi-empirical models in spaceborne GNSS-R for soil moisture retrievals and other terrestrial applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Despite significant progress in point cloud–based 3D building reconstruction, the effectiveness of existing methods remains constrained by four major bottlenecks. These include the scarcity of high-quality annotated datasets, the pervasive incompleteness and noise in real-world point clouds, the substantial memory consumption incurred by the edge search space, and the lack of adequate geometric constraints in network designs, which often causes loss functions to fail in capturing the underlying 3D structural regularities. To address these challenges, we pre-train a self-supervised learning (SSL) framework on large-scale unlabeled point clouds to learn generic geometric representations, and subsequently fine-tune it using only a limited set of annotated roof samples for 3D wireframe reconstruction. The key contribution of this work is the proposal of a new SSL-based framework that utilizes geometry aware edge point identification to facilitate wireframe reconstruction and avoid missing corners, thereby mitigating issues related to dataset quality and computational complexity. Specifically, the framework first leverages a pretrained SSL-based feature extractor to generate robust point-wise representations under sparse and imperfect inputs. Building on these features, an edge point–guided regression module predicts the parametric attributes of roof wireframes. To effectively supervise the training of this regression module, we formulate a joint loss function that optimizes two complementary objectives: (1) minimizing the Euclidean distance between input points and their nearest line segments, and (2) reducing the distance between candidate edge points and their projections on the reconstructed model. Coupled with bipartite edge matching and an edge-aware non-maximum suppression strategy, the proposed method ensures geometric validity while reducing computational redundancy. Extensive experiments across three datasets of varying geometric complexity and scale (Dense, Building3D, and the large-scale Tallinn City dataset) demonstrate the framework’s effectiveness. The SSL-based pretrained model attains 97% corner precision and 91% edge precision for wireframe reconstruction on the Building3D dataset, surpassing conventional baselines and approaching the performance of fully supervised methods. Remarkably, even under limited supervision, specifically utilizing only 50% partially labeled data during fine-tuning, the model maintains strong accuracy with 86% corner precision and 67% edge precision, underscoring its advantages over current SSL-based approaches.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent advances in remote sensing (RS) have improved panchromatic (PAN) and multispectral (MS) data acquisition, but challenges remain in multi-source fusion classification due to limitations in non-redundant feature extraction (i.e., effectively preserving complementary information while suppressing source-specific redundancies) and cross-source alignment (i.e., accurately matching spatial–spectral correspondences between PAN and MS data). The emerging state space model (SSM)-based Mamba model, with its global modeling and linear complexity, shows potential for complex land cover classification tasks. To this end, we propose MMC-Net, a novel Mamba-based multi-level class-aware network for PAN and MS fusion classification, which integrates a progressive fusion feature learning (PFFL) module and a multi-branch class-aware classification (MBCAC) module. The PFFL module employs dual-path spatial–spectral Mamba (S 2 Mamba) to extract discriminative features while suppressing redundancy through the feature reconstruction module (FRM), along with fusion Mamba (FMamba) to enable cross-source interaction via channel exchange and Fusion SSM. The MBCAC module enhances classification diversity through spatial-wise softmax and multi-branch learning, while its parameter-sharing mechanism across levels captures hierarchical discriminative patterns. Validated on three PAN-MS and one HSI-LiDAR datasets, MMC-Net achieves state-of-the-art performance and demonstrates robust, source-agnostic generalization across heterogeneous modalities. The code is available at https://github.com/Xidian-AIGroup190726/MMCNet .

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generalized multi-label zero-shot learning predicts seen and unseen labels from a predefined vocabulary, yet independent image-text matching leaves label relations unused. We propose Score-TEGAR, which refines frozen GeoRSCLIP scores through a typed label-relation graph. Seen-label statistics define co-occurrence, exclusion, and hierarchy; text affinity connects unseen labels. The Typed-Edge Graph Attention Refiner (TEGAR) combines relation-specific propagation, a non-positive exclusion residual, and uncertainty-aware scaling. Across MLRSNet, BigEarthNet, and China-MAS-50k, it achieved the highest mean harmonic mean of seen and unseen mean average precision (mAP-HM) among thirteen configurations: 0.614, 0.439, and 0.476. Parameter-matched controls support the contribution of typed graph structure, while label-level analysis reveals uneven benefits across unseen categories. Score-space refinement provides a route to structured prediction when candidate labels are known but training annotations are incomplete.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mountain vegetation dynamics serve as sensitive indicators of environmental change, making accurate fractional cover mapping of primary components crucial for understanding ecosystem responses. This task has been facilitated by multispectral satellite imagery, particularly Sentinel-2, which provides continuous observations. To translate these observations into fractional cover maps, existing studies typically apply fraction estimation models to synthetic spectra derived from field-measured signatures. However, limited field surveys in mountain terrain present a data bottleneck for synthetic spectra construction, while complex vegetation composition introduces spectral ambiguities that existing methods struggle to represent. Therefore, this study develops a knowledge-guided unmixing framework for annual mapping of the fractional cover of herbaceous vegetation, woody vegetation, and bare land in mountain environments using Sentinel-2 time series. Specifically, to overcome data bottleneck, we develop a knowledge-guided strategy that automatically builds adaptive physio-phenological feature spaces to extract representative signatures. To resolve spectral ambiguity, we incorporate nonlinear mixing mechanisms to synthesize spectra that better represent complex mixing relationships. The synthetic library was then used to train Unmix-Net, a spectral-temporal regression model for fraction estimation. The framework was applied to the central Qilian Mountains and southern Hengduan Mountains spanning broad climatic gradients, validated with high-resolution UAV data. It achieved MAEs of 12.38 %, 8.58 %, and 9.82 % for herbaceous, woody, and bare land, outperforming existing models by up to 7.80 %. When implemented across landscapes ranging from arid steppes and alpine meadows to tropical rainforests, the framework maintained comparable performance. The comparative experiments further confirmed that the proposed extraction strategy improved the balance of component estimates, while combining complementary mixing mechanisms reduced estimation errors. By integrating domain knowledge into data-driven learning, this framework supports spatially continuous fractional vegetation mapping in mountain environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Passive microwave (PMW) remote sensing is a significant data source for generating spatiotemporally continuous land surface temperature (LST). Although empirical methods can retrieve PMW LST that approximates the physical meaning of thermal infrared (TIR)-derived LST, they generally fail to fully consider land-air variables implied by PMW radiative transfer principles. Particularly, the impacts of atmospheric situation have often been overlooked. Here, we propose a machine learning-based retrieval method that integrates a broader range of land-air environmental variables in accordance with PMW radiative transfer principles (PLST-ILA). Validation against pre-extracted MODIS LST shows that the proposed method achieves annual daytime and nighttime root mean square errors (RMSEs) of 3.17 K and 2.21 K, respectively, showing reductions of 9.9 %∼26.1 % relative to the five reference methods. Validation at six in-situ sites indicates that PLST-ILA yields an annual daytime RMSE of 4.21 K and a nighttime RMSE of 4.02 K, reducing the daytime RMSE by 0.33 ∼ 1.68 K and the nighttime RMSE by 0.27 ∼ 1.32 K benchmarked by four advanced methods. The monthly-scale model generally achieves superior performance compared with the seasonal-scale and annual-scale models. Moreover, accounting for topographic and geomorphological characteristics, anthropogenic emissions, atmospheric humidity, and aerosol optical depth may further improve retrieval accuracy. Finally, the proposed method demonstrates the capability to reconstruct PMW observations within orbital gaps. Validation against gap-retained MODIS LST yields annual RMSEs of 3.68 K during the daytime and 2.69 K at night. The results of this study provide a technical reference for PMW LST retrieval and may contribute to optimizing the precision of thermal-related research.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Agroforestry is a land-use practice that integrates trees into agricultural lands, supporting food security and rural livelihoods while delivering both climate adaptation and mitigation benefits. However, current large-scale assessments often approximate the spatial extent of agroforestry using coarse tree canopy cover or biomass products that cannot characterize tree spatial arrangements within croplands, potentially obscuring structurally distinct agroforestry systems. Here, we produced a 100 m resolution map of the spatial extent of agroforestry lands in Africa for 2019, using 10 m resolution tree canopy cover data derived from PlanetScope, together with Sentinel-2 imagery. This map explicitly delineates croplands where agroforestry systems are practised by distinguishing tree spatial patterns within croplands, focusing on irregularly scattered trees and trees aligned in rows. Scattered trees were identified using tree-pixel distance metrics, whereas trees planted in rows were detected using a U-Net model trained with ∼38,000 manually labelled tree-row features. The identified agroforestry lands with scattered trees and trees in rows on cropland were validated against ground-based sites collected from the literature and Google Earth imagery, yielding an overall accuracy of 77.2%. This agroforestry dataset with explicit spatial tree arrangements can serve as a baseline for assessing the environmental impacts and changes in crop yield associated with agroforestry ecosystems, as well as for supporting the development of process-based agroforestry models.

Abstract (英文): Accurate geolocation in GNSS-denied mountainous environments remains a significant challenge for navigation and field operations. While vision-based methods using terrain features like skylines offer a promising passive solution, their performance is often hindered by unknown camera orientation, low feature discriminability across vast areas, and severe degradation under nighttime conditions. To address these limitations, this paper presents a novel visual geolocation framework that synergistically couples ephemeris-based orientation correction with multi-scale skyline feature matching. First, an Edge-Enhanced SegFormer-B3 (EESFormer-B3) network is developed for robust skyline extraction from query images, where a lightweight Edge Gate mechanism is integrated to refine boundary precision, particularly under low-light scenarios. Second, we propose a camera heading estimation method utilizing solar and lunar ephemeris, eliminating reliance on external heading sensors and drastically reducing the matching search space. Third, for matching, a hierarchical composite feature is designed, encoding the skyline’s global shape trends, local directional variations, and dominant structural distributions to ensure robustness against repetitive terrain patterns. Extensive experiments were conducted in a complex 700 km 2 mountainous region. The results demonstrate that our method achieves a localization success rate of 82 % (daytime) and 76 % (nighttime) within a 250-meter error threshold. Compared with a full 360° circular shift search, the proposed ephemeris-based correction reduces the average matching time from approximately 60 s to 2 s, corresponding to a speedup of more than 30 times. This work provides a reliable, efficient, and illumination-invariant geolocation solution, with strong potential for applications in autonomous navigation, search and rescue, and other field missions in GNSS-denied natural environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Quantifying greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions from livestock operations is a prerequisite for reconciling bottom-up inventories with atmospheric observations. However, global GHG reporting remains limited by a lack of spatially explicit data on animal waste management systems (AWMS), forcing reliance on coarse regional assumptions rather than operation-level observations. Existing geospatial foundation models partly address this spatial gap by mapping cattle-operation footprints that provide upstream spatial context, but contextual detection alone cannot identify the manure-management features within them. To address this gap, we introduce the Bovine Slurry Index (BSI), an empirical Sentinel-2 spectral index for detecting liquid/slurry manure ponds within known cattle-operation footprints. The primary formulation, BSI-E, uses calibrated narrow-NIR (B8A) and SWIR-1 (B11) reflectance bounds derived from manure pond samples in the United States and China. The index targets the empirically observed intermediate NIR-SWIR response of manure ponds relative to clear water, vegetation, dry holding pens, and exposed soil. Calibration used 2,730 pond samples from the U.S. and China, and segmentation was evaluated against 945 reference polygons across all five geographic regions. BSI-E produced stronger pond-to-background contrast and higher segmentation accuracy than evaluated water indices, with dataset-level intersection-over-union (IoU) ranging from 0.26 in Brazil to 0.68 in the United States and precision reaching 0.91. Optimal thresholds varied geographically from 0.25 to 1.00, and performance was strongest in dryland feedlot systems and lower in humid or spectrally mixed settings. BSI provides a target-specific spectral method for generating operation-scale evidence that could inform AWMS activity data within these footprints.

</details>
